ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u28	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Minister! Ensuring the security of our country's citizens is one of the important goals that the government led by Prime Minister Beata Szydło has set itself. The sense of security has been greatly reduced in many towns, as in recent years numerous police stations and police stations have been eliminated. On 26 September the police station in Chmielnik was reopened in Świętokrzyskie Voivodeship. Unfortunately, the former precinct was wound up in 2010. The fact of the return of police officers was very well received by both the inhabitants of Chmielnik and local authorities. With this in mind, I ask you to answer the question: How many police stations or police stations were liquidated in the years 2008 to 2015 and how is the process of restoring police stations or police stations in small towns progressing? What will be the next decisions on this matter in the Ministry of Home Affairs? Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u29	Thank you very much. I understand that I can ask the Minister for an answer. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u30	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! (PL) Mr President, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, Between 2008 and 2015, the PO-PSL coalition, which was then in power, closed down more than half of the stations operating in our country. At the end of 2007, there were 817 of them. When we took power as Law and Justice after the election, we found only 399 police stations, so the 418 were eliminated. We, as Members of the Law and Justice, protested in our previous term of office. But also some Members of the then government coalition disagreed. We didn't agree to keep the police from the citizen. The government didn't listen to this, it wasn't listened to the chief chief at the time, it wasn't listened to by the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration of the Civic Platform. This was the idea of the police on the phone. It was assumed that if there was an incident, there would be a police call, she would come quickly, react and everything would be fine. We warned against it because the police on the phone didn't work anywhere and we knew it wouldn't work in Poland. It was assumed that there would be financial savings here. We asked him what the title was. The answers were very enigmatic. And these savings turned out to be illusory, and the consequences of this mass liquidation were such that the sense of security of citizens in municipalities, in smaller cities, fell. Police forces have been concentrated mainly in city, county, and exposed, so to speak, to the criminals of smaller towns and people living there. Law and Justice have always believed that we must take care of security equally - that it is necessary that it is the responsibility of the state - residents of all Poland, as large agglomerations as smaller towns. And we announced that when we took power, when we won the election, we would re-enact the police stations. And that's what we're doing right now. Numerous consultations took place in early 2016 across the country. Within three months, from 21 January to 21 April, about 12 thousand consultations were held in Poland. There was a great interest of residents in these meetings, took part in them approx. Twenty-two thousand. citizens. One of the postulates that repeated itself was the demand to re-establish police stations where they were eliminated. We have conducted a very thorough analysis of the case and, as a result of it, using specific criteria, we have established - these are data for today - that 156 stations need to be rebuilt, but this list is obviously not closed. At the same time, it is important to make it clear that we should abolish - we also said that then, I said this in particular as Vice-President of the then Committee on Home Affairs and Administration in the Sejm, and other Members of the Law and Justice said - it is easy, it will be harder to reproduce. And it was said that there was trouble, because objects, especially those that served police stations, were destined for other purposes at that time, otherwise developed, given to someone, sold. They just don't exist. In the first place, So now we're reconstructing stations where there's room capacity. Cooperation with local authorities in this area is very good. Besides, local authorities protested in those years against the liquidation of police stations. Very often. After all, they put money into the operation of these posts, and then someone without consulting them, contrary to their position, took them away, eliminated them. Now, thanks to the cooperation with local governments that helped us prepare our facilities, we have already recreated 18 stations on a national scale in the last weeks, 21 more are ready to reactivate by the end of this year. Another of this list - 156 - will be played in the coming months, perhaps even years, because there are cases where there is no facility at all where the police station could be located. And you may even have to build these objects. In the draft Modernization Act, which was prepared by the Ministry of Home Affairs and Administration for the years 2017-2020, we provided financial resources for, among other things, the construction of police stations where there is no possibility of developing existing facilities. And this process - therefore - will continue for a longer time. But where, I repeat, once again, there will be room opportunities, we will reactivate these stations faster, in a continuous process this is happening right now. The police provide everything they need to operate the stations. On the other hand - once again I want to emphasize this - local governments, for which we are grateful, help in preparing objects. We want these precincts to work not eight hours, but much longer, preferably 24 hours a day. And that is what we are going to do when it comes to civil protection, so that they include the protection of the residents of the municipalities, the towns around the clock. But of course in a flexible way, because there are periods in different localities when you need to increase the presence of police, because mass events are held, e.g. discotheques, whether these are tourist areas. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u34	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On 8 March 1990, the Polish Parliament adopted one of the most important laws of the state - the law on local government. It was a historic event, because after 40 years there was a restitution of local governments in Poland, which was an important modernization step in the process of decentralisation of public administration. It was then introduced for many years of territorial order. After several years of territorial stability, municipalities and powiats began to develop trends in changing the boundaries of certain municipalities. In many cases, these changes were justified and were supported by the authorities constituting the municipalities concerned. But there were also examples of repeated changes in borders following in the years to come. Such changes in borders took place in the case of the town of Rzeszów. The Voivodeship city of Rzeszów started the process of changing its borders in 2006. Further changes were made by 2010 each year. Rzeszów has expanded its surface more than twice: from 54 km2 to 116 km2. These changes were accompanied by an increase in the population, but only by a dozen percent. In this way Rzeszów became one of the lowest population density. There was also a dangerous phenomenon for the development of cities so-called. Spilling the city. In parallel, however, the change of borders was accompanied by a reduction in the economic potential, the development potential of the municipalities that gave up their territories. In law and justice policy, there is an assumption that [[♪ The bell ♪]] all local government entities developed evenly. In this context, how does the Ministry see the expansion of large cities at the expense of small, less affluent municipalities? Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u35	Thank you, sir. I'm asking the Minister to answer.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u36	Mr. Marshal! Mr. Speaker! Ladies and gentlemen! The Minister for Home Affairs and Administration shall be responsible for carrying out border-setting activities. According to the Ministry of Development, the creation of instruments to encourage cooperation between neighbouring local government units is an appropriate form to create conditions for the development of these areas. In the current financial perspective of 2014-2020, action is being taken to mobilise JST for dialogue and cooperation. Currently realized so-called. Integrated territorial investment strengthens the forms of cooperation of local authorities in the functional areas of cities. The work currently under way on the Responsible Development Strategy provides for the optimisation of the implementation of the ZITs and the conversion of unions, the ZITs into sustainable partnerships of JSTs after the completion of the 2020 financial perspective to create metropolitan development and coordinate the actions of municipalities entering into the partnership. However, it should be noted that the expansion of large cities must not result from the desire to acquire new areas for investment, housing or leisure activities. Such an understanding of the desire to expand the area of the city may result in a strengthening of the adverse phenomenon of uncontrolled expansion of buildings in these areas. In the National Urban Policy 2023, which is also reflected in the strategy for responsible development, emphasis is placed on the need for a compact, sustainable city, which is conducive to reducing the phenomenon of suburbanisation with many adverse effects. The expansion of urban areas at the expense of neighbouring municipalities should not be motivated by the decline in urban population and their transfer to suburban municipalities. The current law on the income of local government units allows the municipality resulting from the merger of two or more municipalities by means of concerted resolutions to increase the rate of participation in income tax revenues from individuals by 5 percentage points over a period of 5 years, starting on 1 January of the year following the year in which the decision to merge was taken. One example of the municipalities that joined as a result of the agreement was the connection between the municipality of Zielona Góra and the rural municipality of Zielona Góra in 2014. In summary, it should be pointed out that urban expansion activities are self-government initiatives and each case requires individual consideration depending on local circumstances and impact on development opportunities. These proceedings shall be conducted in accordance with the applicable legislation. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u71	I would like to thank Mr Naimski for his reply.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u72	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! Maybe a few general statements first. The fact is that the National Energy System requires investment. This is a question of security of energy supply in our country. These investments are high voltage 400 kV transmission lines, linear investments. What does that mean? This means that the construction of each new line requires the passage through many municipalities on quite long stretches, often in violation of the obvious interests of local communities, as well as specific owners of parcels or houses by which or near which these investments must be carried out. And it requires, of course, gentleness, it requires caution, it requires consultation, it requires compromises, and it also costs a lot of money, because sometimes you just have to pay compensation for used terrain, private territory. The 400 kV line from Kozienice is a line that will allow power output, energy from the newly built power plant in Kozienice. This plant in Kozienice will be put into service in 2018. In order to make full use of the capacity of this new power plant, it is necessary to build a new energy line to enable this. This line has been planned in such a way that the energy from Kozienice will be used in a large part, among others. in the Warsaw agglomeration. Thus many years ago, a project of the line from Kozienice to the vicinity of Warsaw was created. What is happening at the moment about this investment is related to this, I will say in general, that the areas around Warsaw are very difficult investment areas, when it comes to linear investment, because they are very densely populated, are densely built. The infrastructure there is already quite complicated. The projects that we found at the beginning of this year with the investor in the Polish Power Networks, but also with the contractor, are projects that were already at a multi-option stage for the reasons that I already mentioned, i.e.. local conflicts with regard to the specific course of the line. A really deep analysis of all these options has been carried out and it has turned out that the most rational is to run a new line along the communication lines, that this will be the least interfering. However, it also turned out that the number of social protests related to this route is so large that the investor has returned to the line which is decided in a formal sense, i.e. is included in the spatial development plan of the Mazowieckie voivodship adopted a few years ago. The 2015 resolution, which you mentioned, is a resolution that opens up work on the modification of this plan. This has never been taken, so there is a variant that exists in the spatial development plan of the Mazowieckie voivodship. I just want to add one more thing that might be important. The Polish Power Networks continue to conduct a very detailed audit of this investment and the entire planning of this investment over many, many years in PSE. There must be a line that connects Kozienice to the energy line, by agreement - I do not know if the honourable Member of the House has a map in front of his eyes at the moment - in any case it will connect Kozienice with a line of high voltage between the Warsaw and Łódź agglomeration, because it closes the energy ring around Warsaw. These analyses continue to be conducted multi-optionally also in the sense that the credibility of the selected investor is being examined and decision-making procedures are being examined in the last few years within the Polish Power Networks. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u80	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! Also at the beginning of the year, in March, before the SIAL fair in Canada, we invited entrepreneurs to consult, to provide information on the benefits of CETA. Only 10 entrepreneurs came forward, and this interest is simply small. This may be due to the fact that our trade flows with Canada represent a very small fraction of total exports. This is only less than €1.5 billion. I would also like to make it very clear that we have a very positive trade balance with Canada: we export goods for EUR 1.1 billion, we import only EUR 300 million, while the structure of trade is very beneficial - we export furniture, rubber, clothing, of course in a large part also food products. This indicates that this is an export generated by Polish companies, not e.g. as part of transfers between foreign capital groups. I would therefore also like to ensure that we consult widely. As far as the provisional application is concerned, I stress once again that the Polish government has not yet agreed to anything, because there is no official position. On the other hand, we are pleased to note that CETA has been recognised as a mixed agreement, which requires the consent of all Member States and not just of the European Parliament. That's what we've been paying attention to from the very beginning. The possibility for the European Union to provisionally apply the CETA trade agreement is set out in Article. 218 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union: CETA's provisions, which fall within the competence of the European Union, i.e. the commercial part in general, may be applied on a provisional basis. On the other hand, the investment part, which is the most controversial issue, which at the moment is also discussed within the Polish government and which concerns the principle of settlement of disputes on the lines of the state - the investor, this part concerning investment courts, does not fall within the scope of provisional application. It will enter into force only after the ratification by the last of the parliaments of the Member States. Therefore, to conclude, I would like to assure you that all these sensitivitys that have been reported both by the public, although I have the impression that the interest of CETA is really small from the public... Of course, I know a number of criticisms, but given the very small trade turnover with Canada, i.e. the resulting small interest of Polish entrepreneurs, it seems that this matter will not cause any significant controversy after the adoption, if this happens, because the final position is not yet decided. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u90	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In answer to the questions raised, I would like to say that according to the draft Act on National Tax Administration and the Act introducing the regulations within the framework of the National Tax Administration, the following organisational units will operate: organizational cells of the Ministry of Finance serving the head of KAS, National Tax Information, tax administration chambers, tax offices, customs and treasury offices, the National Treasury School and the Information Technology Centre of the National Tax Administration. The revised structure will consist of uniform, at both local and regional level, tax administrations. The proposed law, the provisions introducing the law on the KAS, contains regulations that allow the customs chambers to be connected with the tax administrations and the customs offices to be abolished. At the same time, the project provides for the establishment of customs and treasury offices. With regard to the suggestion made regarding the liquidation of the Customs Chamber in Przemyśl, I would like to explain that in the regulations cited above, it follows that the organizational units of the Customs Service will be included in the consolidation. At the same time, I inform you that the conceptual work on the shape of the field structure of the National Tax Administration is still ongoing. The organisational structure of the National Tax Administration is closely linked to the tasks that will be legally assigned to the various KAS organisational units. In view of the above, at this stage of work, it is not possible to provide an unambiguous answer concerning the future seat of the Chamber of Tax Administration in the Podkarpackie Voivodship. The analyses shall take into account both the issues of property ownership, infrastructure and communication, as well as the qualifications of staff employed in tax offices, tax offices and customs offices. As the establishment of the organisational structure of the office will take into account, in addition to the tasks, the economic, organisational and social aspects, I assure you that the argument relating to the important role of Przemyśl in the region will be considered during the decision-making process and these decisions will be taken immediately after the legislative process has been completed. Thank you very much.

Just so we're clear, they're entitled to it, because that's what markets are for, to make money out of them. On the other hand, economies suffer from this, at a time when this speculation is relevant to them, for example. raw materials, it is observed that one day something is cheap, the next day it is expensive. In wholesale markets this causes terrible perturbations, retail markets are affected by entities that try to smooth out these speculative impulses somehow. Ladies and gentlemen, what happened in Polish mining? Using the situation in which we have relatively low carbon prices on the markets - but there are already reasons to expect that this is a transitional situation - we have decided to create a structure that separates us from such fluctuations. Besides, the beginnings of such a structure have appeared before, behind previous teams, we have sort of finished it. Ladies and gentlemen, if someone says that RES energy is free, because the wind blows at no cost, they must be aware that it is not free, because it costs its extraction in the form of using turbines, certain investments. Similarly you can say about coal - it is free as long as it lies in the ground. We need to get him out, and it's costing us. In the vertical integration structure, coal costs are no longer relevant to energy costs. In fact, the cost of mining is the infrastructure in which mining works. Therefore, we can approach planning rationally, we can make thoughtful moves to increase efficiency, we can allocate resources to improve the efficiency of this sector by better organisation, by better investment. As a result, this will result in a lower component of the costs associated with raw materials for the generation of electricity in conventional energy. Therefore, this movement will not translate into an increase in prices, on the contrary - in the medium and long term it will lead to stable prices, so that citizens will not have to wonder and check daily what is happening on energy exchanges. Ladies and gentlemen, the Commission's view is the result of two trends - political and classical economics. In the classical economy, it is known that if prices do not reflect costs, where expenditure of a fixed nature is incurred, they should be transferred to the recipient in the form of fixed charges, where variable factors are, they should be transferred in the form of charges adjusted to the extent of consumption. Each other system is an inefficient system, it can be used to mask certain political concepts, which is partly happening in the European Union by e.g. The introduction of RES sources is a political concept and requires funding from the tariff structure. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u106	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am therefore referring to the issue of the fees laid down in the draft Water Law Act. Provisions establishing an obligation for all water users to pay fees for the use of water resources are essential to fulfilling the obligations imposed by the Water Framework Directive on Poland. In accordance with this Water Framework Directive, it is up to the Member States to implement the obligations set out in Article. 9 of that Directive, the principle of the reimbursement of the costs of water services. This means that national law should not generally exempt water levyers from the obligation to pay fees. In addition, the full implementation of the rules on the reimbursement of water services results from the ex ante condition imposed on Poland by the European Commission under the Operational Programme "Infrastructure and the environment'. And the adoption of the proposal for this law Water Law by 31 December this year provides for payment for water investment in the value of approximately EUR 3.5 million. If we do not introduce fees for all water users and this law is not passed by the end of this year, we will lose this €3.5 billion. The draft Water Law Act is about to comply with the principle of art. 9 of this directive, which says that the user pays, the polluter pays. And in Poland - at present - the reimbursement of water services costs, due to the number of exemptions that exist in the current Environmental Law, means that a relatively small amount of water is charged. And this is what the European Commission is accusing us of. And the aim of the new water financing system is to implement strategic plans. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u115	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you very much for the up-to-date questions about the real work on the bill amending the Social Security Pensions Act in two areas - i.e. in the area of the rules for the valorisation of pension benefits as well as in the area of the rules governing the amount of the lowest pension benefit. In fact, both operations, both changes are due to certain shortcomings in the mechanism used so far to value pension benefits, which was a percentage valorisation. Pension benefits, including benefits paid in the amount of the lowest benefit, which so far is in the case of pensions for total incapacity for work 882,56 zł, were increased by a percentage including two parameters - namely the price index of consumer goods and services and at least 20% a real increase in average wage. It can be said that the application of this percentage has resulted in the benefits of those receiving the lowest pension benefits increasing disproportionately less than those received by those receiving those benefits at a higher level. Thus, on 13 September 2016, the government of Prime Minister Beata Szydło decided that there was a need to correct both mechanisms. At the moment the resort of family, work and social policy has already developed a bill. This draft amending act is in the process of public consultation. The project involves increasing the amount of the lowest pension benefits, as well as the total incapacity pension benefits from the amount mentioned, i.e. from PLN 882.56 to PLN 1 000. zł. This is a significant increase. A similar mechanism will apply to partial incapacity pensions, except that this increase will be relatively lower. This will be an increase from the amount of PLN 676,75 to the amount of PLN 750. And a similar operation will apply to the amount of social pensions, which currently amounts to PLN 741,35 and will increase by nearly PLN 100 to the amount of PLN 840. As regards the second correction, the second mechanism proposed in the draft law, which corrects these deficiencies in the percentage valorisation, consists in determining the amount of the minimum increase in pension benefit; it consists in determining that amount at the level of 10 PLN. It can be said that around 2100 000 will benefit from the introduction of this minimum increase in pension benefits in this mechanism. recipients receiving benefits under the general scheme, as well as almost all recipients receiving benefits under the agricultural social security scheme, as for farmers this will apply to 1,100 000. of the 1,200 000 people. all beneficiaries receiving benefits under the agricultural scheme. With regard to the first mechanism I mentioned - i.e. raising the amount of the lowest pension benefit to PLN 1000 - about 800,000 will be increased as a result of this operation. Pensioners and pensioners receiving benefits under the general scheme, as well as benefits received by 350 000. persons entitled to benefits in the agricultural system. All social benefits will increase as a result of this operation. About 285,000. persons will receive these benefits at a higher level. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u120	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! In recent years, there has been an unprecedented increase at the external borders of the European Union in the number of immigrants and refugees who are trying to enter the European Union. The exclusive responsibility for border control lies with countries whose border is the external border of the European Union. On 1 May 2005 Frontex was set up under a Council Regulation, the full name of which is: the European Agency for the Management of Operational Cooperation at the External Borders of the Member States of the European Union and one of the tasks of which is to provide additional technical assistance to those countries of the European Union which are under intense migratory pressure. The Agency may provide additional equipment, e.g. aircraft, cars, boats, and deploy trained border staff to those countries which require such support. I have a question for the Minister here. What is our cooperation with Frontex in those areas that represent the responsibilities of the Agency? ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u121	Thank you, Member. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u122	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, First of all, I would like to thank you very much. But that's an important question. I would like to inform you that cooperation with the European Agency for the Management of Operational Cooperation at the External Borders of the Member States of the European Union - Frontex - is multifaceted and concerns elements such as the coordination of operational cooperation between Member States in the field of the management of external borders, including the provision of support to Member States in situations where technical and operational assistance is needed at the external borders. Secondly, it concerns training activities for border guards. Thirdly, carrying out risk analyses. Fourthly, the development of research relevant for the control of external borders. Fifthly, the coordination or organisation of joint return operations - these are refugees. Finally, it concerns the implementation of information systems that allow for the exchange of information on emerging threats at the external borders, including EUROSUR. It should be stressed here that the key form of cooperation in the context of the current migration crisis, as mentioned by the Member, is however the participation in operational activities at land, sea and air borders coordinated by Frontex. The head of the Border Guard is the competent authority in Poland in the field of cooperation with the Agency. The missions of Border Guard officers in joint operations coordinated by Frontex shall be organised in accordance with previous Border Guard declarations in the framework of negotiations which bear the ABT symbol, namely the Annual Bilateral Talks, as well as in response to ad hoc requests from the Agency, called calls, to provide support in border situations which could not have been previously foreseen. Joint operational actions coordinated by Frontex shall be implemented by organising support missions in the points and sectors of the external borders of the European Union identified on the basis of a risk analysis. As part of the mission, support officers from different Member States shall be included in the teams of local border services. The importance of missions carried out using specialised technical equipment such as e.g. mobile surveillance units or aviation equipment that complement the host country's own hardware capabilities of the joint operation. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to assure you that Poland is committed and supported by Frontex, whose primary objective is to protect the external borders of the European Union, which is one of the key measures to combat the migration crisis and the condition necessary to preserve the integrity of the Schengen area. This commitment in our assessment is more than standard and goes beyond the level of involvement of other Member States in the European Union. This is in line with the policy that we are pursuing, which puts the protection of the European Union's external borders first. Poland continues not only to maintain but to intensify the support given to Frontex to deal with and combat the immigration crisis. As regards specific data, as at 30 September 2016, 85 Border Guard officers participate in joint operations coordinated by Frontex, including 39 in Greece, 32 in Bulgaria, 14 Border Guard officers were seconded to Romania, Spain and Italy, Latvia, Hungary and Slovakia. In addition, five Border Guard officers were seconded to Frontex as national experts. Another seven officers carry out tasks in other EU missions. I wanted to stress that this is absolutely not the end, and our commitment will be increased. Between July and September 2016, Polish Border Guard officers served at land, sea and air borders as part of joint border operations coordinated by Frontex. At land borders, focal points have been established as a permanent platform for professional support, exchange of experience and training in order to synchronise activities related to the control and surveillance of external air, land and sea borders, enabling the integration of border management. The focal points constitute a permanent platform for carrying out border actions of a permanent nature as well as an infrastructure base for regional time actions. The Border Guard shall participate in the WO Focal Points land-based component both as a host country for foreign guest officers' missions and as an organizer of border guards' missions in other Member States. Technical equipment is also used in the activities, e.g. Mobile surveillance units, helicopters, cars, thermal imaging equipment. In the field of cooperation at land borders, the involvement of Poland in the joint operation Flexible Operational Activities 2016 in Bulgaria deserves particular attention. At sea borders, however, it is particularly important to involve Poland in the joint operation Poseidon 2016 in Greece and Triton 2016 in Italy. In addition, the Border Guard is also involved in operations in Spain. The purpose of the air operations is to strengthen airports, to meet weaknesses in the area of border protection at State airports. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The time is running, but I will say that on 14 September 2016 the European Union regulation entered into force on 6 October, which will transform Frontex into a European Border and Coast Guard Agency with much broader competences. And, of course, the headquarters of Frontex, as you all know, is in Warsaw. The Polish government has been obliged to negotiate the maintenance of the seat of the future agency and these negotiations are currently being conducted, with a very high pace and good course, as is acknowledged by the representatives of Frontex, the Frontex authorities, as well as the European Commission.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u217	Thank you very much. Mr Marcin Święcicki will present the position of the Civic Platform Club. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u218	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Comprehensive Economic and Trade Agreement with Canada was, as has been said, negotiated and signed, initialled by the negotiators two years ago. It is fully available, there is a Polish text so that you can read this agreement. As far as trade with Canada is concerned, this represents around 10% trade turnover in the European Union, but only some less than 0,5% Polish turnover, because Canada is not a great partner for us, so this agreement does not apply to us to the extent that it affects other European countries closer to the Atlantic. As far as our exports to Canada are concerned, we can count on at least such branches as the aviation industry, because we have investors in Poland from Canada, furniture, Polish food, various other products. On the other hand, Europe imports less processed goods when it comes to imports. Canada is such a good partner here that it is a highly developed country, with high revenues and so on. our agriculture has managed in the European Union, and the Canadian market will also have a much wider open market, open to highly processed food. There are raised problems with agriculture itself. Well, agriculture is the only sector where, however, this agreement provides for certain restrictions and the leaving of duties and quotas in some cases. On the most sensitive sections, e.g. in the import of wheat, maize, beef, pigmeat, those amounts that were set for imports into the European Union are, I would say symbolic, reaching some, I don't know, 1% consumption in the European Union, and therefore cannot pose a significant threat to our farmers in this regard. There are concerns about consumer protection. All goods exported to the European Union must meet the European requirements for ingredients of products, their presentation, so-called. Lebeling, that is, what the consumer learns about the product. It will not be possible, for example. exporting to the European Union beef or pigmeat, the production of which will be driven by hormones which cannot be used in the European Union. This also applies to GMOs. We fully retain our European regulations on GMOs and national regulations, because here we have a possibility to regulate this within each country. So there won't be any danger here either. It is sometimes argued that American goods will flow through Canada, even in one of the analyses of the Sejm Analysis Office. In so far as the argument is not met, the rules on the origin of goods apply in this trade agreement, as in all agreements governed by the rules of the World Trade Organisation. Canadian goods exported to the European Union may contain only a certain maximum contribution of foreign imports from the USA or Mexico. It must be primarily a commodity made up of Canadian products. Concerns regarding the settlement of investment disputes are raised. Ladies and gentlemen, I would like to say that we now have an agreement with Canada, which also contains provisions. on the settlement of investment disputes. What is foreseen in CETA is much more, I would say, civilized and developed. Currently, the arbitration panel is appointed by a company that has entered into a dispute. There will be no ad hoc courts where the company will appoint arbitrators, but there will be permanent arbitrators appointed by the states - five by Canada, five by the European Union and five jointly appointed from third countries. Secondly, there will be an appeal procedure. Thirdly, there will be a full transparency of the work of these courts. In total, this way of settling disputes between investors and the state is better than the one we have with Canada so far, and, I would say, does not pose significant threats to the sovereignty of the state, and they also fall, quite absurd, arguments. May I just remind you that we have had such an agreement with the United States since 1990. On the basis of this agreement there were fewer than 10 cases of the Polish State's actions, and at that time, since 1990 the rules in Poland have changed incredibly. In addition, most of these cases Poland won - in one case there was a settlement agreement, one unless we lost. Thanks to this agreement to protect investors from the United States, we received many billions of dollars from US investments, many jobs were created. So for us, the formula that we're going to protect, we're not going to discriminate against foreign investors, that they're going to have protection here, and that, if there's a decision by an international court, it's beneficial because it guarantees investors, it ensures that they're coming in here. Concerns are raised regarding the Regulatory Cooperation Forum. This forum for regulatory cooperation cannot impose any party, Canada or the European Union, any standards, any standards. All the principles of European standards will remain in the same procedure as they are now, while the Forum aims at exchanging information, consulting, working together to ensure that standards and standards are as uniform as possible in the future. For example, there will be electric cars, drones or other things, and the idea is that there will be one American-European market, because if each of these commercial areas has its own separate regulations, it will increase costs and for producers, and of course for consumers, and reduces overall prosperity. So this forum serves to exchange information, cooperation, while it does not change the system of approval of standards and standards on both sides. This will also allow access to public procurement in Canada, also at federal level, which may be important for our companies. It is also worth mentioning that thanks to this agreement, many ways of allowing the market - through checks, certificates, consents, tests, tests - will be common, it will not be necessary to repeat these tests to enter the American market. European tests will be recognised in Canada, which will in particular facilitate the entry of smaller and medium-sized enterprises into that market, because for them this barrier of double testing is particularly, I would say, costly and difficult to overcome. Finally, I would like to conclude by saying that it is also worth paying attention to the strategic aspect. Canada is a member of NATO, like us, but strengthening economic cooperation also strengthens strategic security, strategic cooperation and strategic solidarity. Let us remember that Canada is also very active in economic and political cooperation with countries on the other side of the Pacific, with very dynamically developing countries in the Far East. For us, maintaining Canada's commitment and Canada's economic cooperation with Europe is also important for this strategic reason - so that this partner feels closely connected with Europe with its economic interests. I will also mention that there is a large Polish minority, Canadian Polonia. For her this is also an opportunity - easier establishing economic cooperation with Poland will also be an opportunity for her. In total, the Civic Platform will support the signing of this agreement and its provisional application until it is adopted in the manner required for mixed contracts. I will also say that the adoption of a mixed agreement by all national states means approval by 36 national and regional parliaments, because in some countries it must also be regionally approved, so it is a very long procedure. Therefore, as regards this commercial part, which is within the Commission's competence, I believe that we should give consent to this to the European Council so that this part enters into force as soon as possible. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u236	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Members' Circle of Free and Solidarnia, I present my position on draft resolutions on TTIP and CETA international agreements, prints Nos 727, 728, 831 and 858. The draft resolutions submitted are addressed to the Council of Ministers and to the Minister of Foreign Affairs, expressing the intention to take an official position representing Polish interests, highlighting the subjectivity of the Polish state. With regard to the resolutions submitted, there are important legal questions to be settled. The European Commission has negotiated the conclusion of these agreements under Article for 5 years. 207 and 218 of the Treaty of Lisbon. It follows that the Polish side can only use the instruments provided for in these articles in foreign policy. According to Art. 207 (2) is the European Parliament and the Council, acting by means of regulations, in accordance with the ordinary legislative procedure, adopt measures laying down the framework for the implementation of the common commercial policy. As regards the negotiation and conclusion of agreements, the Council of the European Union shall adopt them by a qualified majority. This requires active policies in all EU bodies and, as a last resort, the construction of blocking minorities in the Council of the European Union. It can be considered that if the Treaties in question fall within the area of trade and services, as well as within the sphere of culture, which could jeopardise the cultural and linguistic diversity of the Union, and in the field of trade in social services and in the field of education and health, which could significantly distort the operation of the national system of these services and have a negative impact on the responsibility of the Member States for providing them, then Poland would be able to effectively exercise the right of veto. In addition, Article. 218 gives the Member States and the European Parliament, the Council of the European Union and the Commission a great deal of opportunity to interfere with the content of the trade treaties and, as a last resort, allows the Court of Justice to ask for an opinion on the compatibility of the agreement envisaged with the Treaties, which, in the case of a negative opinion of the Court, blocks the entry into force of the agreement. In this context, possible use of Article. 90 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland to block TTIP or CETA by the Polish Parliament seems unreasonable because Poland has already adopted the Treaty of Lisbon in this way and has thus delegated its sovereignty in the field of common trade policy to the EU institutions. The dilemma of whether these agreements are mixed or exclusive competence of the Union may be secondary when contracts can be implemented temporarily for many years. The multi-annual ratification procedure by all EU countries will be so extended that TTIP and CETA agreements will actually apply and there will be no real possibility of leaving them. In addition, the application of the ratification mechanism by all parliaments could lead to a fragmentation in the European Union and its division into the Union of different speeds or completely paralyze the decision-making mechanism by placing the European project in a lost position. With this scenario, Poland is threatened by marginalisation. Does the Polish government have a strategy for this eventuality? On the other hand, the patriotic attitude presented by the Members of the applicants and the substantive arguments that express the legitimate concerns of our compatriots are noteworthy. In particular, the ISDS mechanism mentioned in the draft resolution, the dispute resolution mechanism on the lines of the investor - a country that strengthens the position of international corporations, lack of reliable information on the way genetically modified food is authorised on the Community market and the verification of production standards in the food industry in a non-discriminatory manner of Polish farmers and entrepreneurs, and the lack of reliable information on the impact of negotiated agreements on the Polish economy. According to the optimistic scenario of the European Commission, Polish economic benefits are on the verge of statistical error and are in the final position among the Member States. The Free and Solidarni Circle shares this argument. However, emotional calls and the position that the CETA Agreement covers matters falling within the competence of the Member States of the European Union and should be treated as a mixed agreement cannot replace a sound analysis of benefits and risks. Global security needs to be well understood. Poland is a participant in the Atlantic security system, which guarantees us state sovereignty and civilization development. The common economic area links the Atlantic community even more, and therefore all efforts to develop trade and investment cooperation should be encouraged. Calling on government to act decisively [[♪ The bell ♪]] protecting Polish interests is most appropriate, because the government has instruments of influence on the European Union. In addition, Polish MEPs instead of wasting time on idle discussions about the alleged threat of democracy in Poland should double their attention to the detailed provisions of the Treaty which minimise Polish risks in economic matters. After examining the reports of the Sejm committees, the Free and Solidarni Circle will support the resolutions submitted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Bravo.]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u334	Thank you. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Mr Fellowships and Mr Fellow Members, I am very pleased to be able to answer so many questions on behalf of the government. I am very pleased that the NETA issue raises such interest, because indeed the Polish government considers this agreement to be extremely important, creating huge opportunities for Polish companies. But we also see a number of threats. It is not that the Polish government uncritically accepts issues related to CETA. We analyse very carefully, not only in the Ministry of Development, but also in all other ministries, the consequences, the potential risks arising from the adoption of CETA. We are aware of these threats. This is one of the reasons why there is no position of the Polish government today. I also immediately wanted to remind you that the position of the Polish government was not presented at the last meeting in Bratislava. It, I hope, will be ready for the next Council of Ministers meeting. The Council of Ministers has already discussed the CETA issue. All the comments made by the ministries are very carefully analysed. I, too, as Deputy Minister for Trade Policy, am extremely involved in the process of social dialogue. I also consulted on the effects of the adoption of CETA with the very critical environments of the new trade treaties, i.e. the institutions, with non-governmental organisations. I take this very seriously and assure you that the interests and security of the Polish state, including in this economic aspect, safety and food standards, are absolutely priority for the Polish government. I cannot imagine the situation - and there will be no situation - in which these threats will be ignored. We still have time to reach a final position. Not all Member States already have this final position. We want to give ourselves time to have all possible analyses, opinions, including critical ones, analyze and make a final decision which will take into account all interests of the Polish state. I'll answer the precise questions. Is the Polish government going to do an information campaign? Let me remind you that this information campaign has been going on for a long time. We've invited entrepreneurs to consult. Very few entrepreneurs came forward before a major trade fair in Canada, only 10. We have also sent a consultation inquiry to employers' organisations, OPZZ-et,.Solidarity in the course of the Ministry of Development's work on preparing the position of the Polish government. We only got one feedback that didn't criticize as a general rule, it makes sense. It was only indicated to take into account the sensitivity that arises in this public debate. The Polish government takes this sensitivity into account and approaches it with all due care. Also in this process... If ceta is adopted, the Polish government will be active, because it will be an extremely important instrument for us to support Polish exporters in order to exploit and maximise the commercial benefits resulting from its adoption. We will support Polish entrepreneurs in this process of increasing exports to Canada. We will involve our institutions, our WPHI, in the future trade representative in Canada, in order to engage more actively in presenting the benefits to Polish entrepreneurs, all the more so that there is enormous potential. Today, exports to Canada are over a billion euros, growing constantly, we have a huge surplus in trade with Canada - 800 million euros. However, what is important, this structure is very good, these are products produced by Polish companies, these are not internal transfers e.g. Canadian corporations, which increase this export, are only exports in a large part of Polish companies. Can the Polish government still negotiate the content of the agreement? The Polish government cannot negotiate the content of the agreement, because the text of the Act, the content of CETA has been closed since 2014. Although I will remind you what we very much do not like and we have raised this issue in many discussions that in the process of so-called. Legal scrubbing has changed the CETA content of investment disputes and the arbitration provisions have been amended in favour of investment courts. Here, too, I would like to make it very clear that we have a lot of skepticism. on the mechanism of investment courts. We see important risks here and we analyse the consequences very carefully, and we analyse them because we are dealing with a system or institutions that are not there, they are just going to be created, and therefore we are not able to address the current situation, the current functioning, but we need to assess the effects of an investment court, which is not known in practice, is just a matter of interpretation. This is why we have a particularly high level of criticism about this investment court mechanism. Therefore, the Polish government will seek all possible EU fora, so that in the investment court there will be a place for the Polish representative, for the Polish arbitrage, which would influence the development of the practice that will be created within the framework of the investment court caselaw. There were such information or allegations that CETA would create opportunities for Canadian corporations to expand. I would like to say that the Polish government will not accept a situation in which CETA or any other trade agreement, such as TTIP, would in future serve to make foreign corporations even bigger, especially at the expense of Polish companies. But let us remember that above all CETA is not identical with TTIP, these are two different trade agreements of a completely different scale, weight and economic impact for the Polish industry. CETA refers to Poland's ability to increase this trade with Canada, exports, which today is marginal on the scale of all Polish exports. That's less than 0.5%. Polish exports and even less when it comes to imports from Canada. In answer to some Members' questions, we estimate that over the next 5 years the cumulative increase in Polish exports will amount to around 450 million euros, of which about 40 million will be allocated to agri-food products. So that these economic benefits are for the Polish country, while their scale and weight for all Polish exports are still small. But it also depends on our efficiency and on public administration, how the Polish public administration will support Polish companies in this export process and whether it will be able to effectively provide access to knowledge, to the resources that are in our scope, in such a way that Polish companies can use it effectively. As regards the analysis of costs, expected benefits and risks, it is such that these benefits are mainly due to increased exports to Poland, access to services, to public procurement in Canada, but also, as we critically approach the mechanism of the investment court, let us remember that Poland has very large investments made in Canada, investments made in Orlen, KGHM and other companies, that this mechanism will act in the other direction as an additional element of protection for our investments in Canada. I hope that other investments will follow these investments as well. It is not that the Polish government is waiting for the position of the Sejm. The Polish government wants to develop the best possible position taking into account all the threats that you have pointed out, which were also pointed out by the non-governmental institutions. We want to make a decision that takes into account all the opinions. Such a question has been raised, what is the position of the various ministers in the ceta case. Each ministry prepares its analysis. The Ministry of Agriculture - on the effects on food safety, the Ministry of Justice - on investment disputes on the state line - the investor, the Ministry of Digitisation - on data protection, so each Ministry of Development is working, and the Ministry of Development consolidates all these opinions and comments in order to develop the final position of the government, and all these comments are taken into account very seriously. As far as quality standards are concerned, this question has been raised very often - and once again I would like to assure you that CETA does not refer to food standards. CETA does not in principle create a mechanism that would cause the influx of genetically modified food into Poland. As far as food standards are concerned, this remains at the exclusive disposal of the Member States. Of course, I know these arguments, in which this hypothetical possibility of creating a gate for genetically modified food is raised, to make it more accessible to the Member States, but, first, this mechanism is not fully defined, it does not know what it would look like, second, it seems that Poland's interests in this respect, defined as opposition to the flow of genetically modified goods, are also in line with the interests of other Member States. There is also resistance against GMOs in France, and it is also very strong in Germany. It seems that we all have the same interest in the European Union to protect security standards. The European consumer is used to this, he values these high quality standards, and therefore it seems that all Member States will have the same interest. Here it was argued that we do not deal with control procedurally, and that is true, because indeed, for example. The grain imported from Ukraine actually goes through various routes, through Slovakia, to Poland. So this is an argument that we absolutely understand. And we see these threats. Therefore, once again, we do not take this agreement uncritically. We see all these threats. But much depends on us, in terms of the efficiency of our services. And one more point. What a guarantee - such questions have arisen - that Canadian producers using GMOs will comply with their information obligations and will not artificially undermine quality standards. However, we need to have confidence in food certificates, safety descriptions, product descriptions. If there are certificates in the export documentation that confirm the non-use of GMOs, there must be a certain level of trust, taking into account nevertheless the high legal standards that apply in Canada. But of course, it's a matter of checking it out. However, once again, I stress: it seems that many Member States are particularly vulnerable when it comes to the issue of potential risks, and CETA itself does not create a mechanism that would allow food standards to be lowered. We're talking about a completely hypothetical situation, one where corporations, for example. Canadians will find a way to handle regulations. But it is a question of the efficiency of our administration, of the EU administration, so as not to give these companies the opportunity to export GMO products to the European Union countries. Therefore, we strongly oppose the introduction of a mechanism that will give us the opportunity to gain some competitive advantages, including large American or Canadian international corporations. I am talking about the US, because there is such a concern that CETA will be such a gateway for American companies to export or invest in Europe, especially if TTIP is to be frozen for years, and much suggests that the TTIP negotiation process is currently frozen. We don't know exactly how this is gonna go down in the next few years, so that's the risk. CETA defines quite precisely, albeit in a risk-free way, the concept of doing a significant part of the business in Canada. This is not a direct mechanism which would make it possible to easily use Canada as a country of origin in order to export US goods to the European Union. However, such risks always exist and these mechanisms need to be observed very carefully. I will merely remind you that the procedure is that if, at the end of October, there is agreement on the acceptance of provisional application, this will be confirmed by the European Parliament in the spring and then this provisional application will begin. So we're going to have about 3 and 4 years -- it's hard to say exactly, because it depends on the efficiency of national parliaments -- the assessment of the economic impact, the assessment of whether all these threats are going to materialise or whether these circumvention gates -- bypassing, because it's not the use of the mechanisms recorded by CETA -- are really emerging. And then the parliaments of the Member States will be able to take into account the practice of applying the trade part in the field of Ceuta and will take appropriate decisions. If these threats materialize, it will certainly be taken into account. The question was whether the European Commission could change the nature of this agreement at any time. The European Commission cannot do this. The European Commission has indeed stood, at a certain point, and, in principle, until today, is in the view that this is the sole competence of the European Commission. Fortunately, this agreement is mixed and national parliaments will be involved in this ratification procedure. From the very beginning Poland has consistently emphasized in every body that the position on the nature of the contract as a mixed one is a position at which we will in principle insist. And I am glad that this is reflected in the procedure. Can CETA lead to the disappearance of small farms? High Izbo, let us remember that there is no trade on such a scale between Poland and Canada. Canadian corporations don't have as much business as Americans, that's a whole different level of threat. Therefore, given that imports from Canada are on a very small scale - 300 million, for Polish conditions, is a negligible value of imports - even if this value doubles, it will still be a minimum value of Polish imports. Therefore, it seems that there is nothing to be afraid of when it comes to certain systemic or structural consequences for the Polish industry. But let us remember that exports of agri-food products are an opportunity for our industry. This is a very competitive market and in the sense of the distribution model very open. This gives Polish entrepreneurs a chance. Here was the question - whether the Polish government will, for example, support Polish healthy food. Indeed, the Polish government has clearly defined the priorities of economic development in Poland through a plan of responsible development. We are no longer interested in economic growth based on low labour costs or foreign capital. We want to promote Polish capital and we want the strength of the growth of Polish development to be higher wages. Therefore, we are not interested in creating mechanisms that would allow Polish companies to compete on foreign markets only at a low cost or low price. We are interested in increasing this added value, we are interested in promoting, in this case in the agri-food sector, this unique value, which Polish agri-food products have, i.e. quality, environmentality. Polish soils are not as polluted as in Western countries, so we need to expose these benefits, the values we have. And this is also a question of the effectiveness of the Polish public administration, how we will position the brand of the Polish economy. And I assure everyone here that the Polish government is doing a lot in this area, so that Polish companies will also benefit from the fact that Poland will be associated with products that are technologically and qualitatively healthy. In this sense CETA creates such mechanisms to exploit the advantages that Polish companies have, which they can boast about. The question was whether CETA could force the European Union to increase imports of agri-food products, meat. There are quotas. The quota for beef is 45,000. t, for pork - 75 000, but these are marginal amounts on the scale of consumption or on the scale of trade in the European Union at all. That's less than 0.5%. This mechanism set out in CETA does not create risks or risks that could change the structure of the economy, the structure of entire sectors. Therefore, we do not see such concerns, because we are talking about values in trade which are negligible or at least not relevant from the point of view of the economy as a whole. Are there any more... [[There isn't.]] [[There isn't.]] I seem to have answered all the questions. There was also a question about the protection of intellectual property in the context of copyright. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u338	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the pleasure and the honour to once again present to you information from the activities of the European Union during the six-month Presidency period, in this case the Dutch Presidency, which took place between January and June 2016. According to the announcements, these were mainly four important areas of Union policy, many initiatives which enjoyed intense, clear support from Poland, but also initiatives which did not gain such support and probably will not gain, which later. These four areas are migration and security, employment, the reform of the euro area and climate and energy. To varying degrees, these areas were activated. Certainly, one of the most strategic, important, key elements was the issue of an appropriate European response to the migration crisis and the problems it raised in terms of internal and external security. This was included in the Presidency's plans for obvious reasons, while the importance of this agenda was raised by external circumstances, by events we have seen at the external borders of the European Union. We are pleased to note that it was the Dutch Presidency that successfully moved the emphasis, the European Union's attention from the internal to the external dimension of the European response to the migration crisis. Today we're just a few more steps away. This is completely in line with Polish expectations and Polish emphasis on this matter, because we do not hide that in this matter we noted at the very beginning of the establishment of this office one of the greatest political tensions in relations between Warsaw and Brussels, Warsaw and some of the capitals of the Member States of the European Union. We've made clear progress. In March, we signed an extremely difficult and fragile, multifaceted agreement between the European Union and Turkey. This agreement resulted in a radical decline in the uncontrolled migration movement in the Aegean Sea, which, in turn, has been a huge risk of political instability for many months not only in the humanitarian disaster, but also in the great anxiety of the entire continent. On the other hand, in accordance with the wish of the European Summit, with general Polish support, in line with Polish expectations, we have set up the European Border and Coast Guard in such a way as to meet the Polish expectations and the Polish deepest conviction as to how the institutions should work, including the Community institutions, which are connected with such a fundamental dimension of sovereignty as the protection of borders and security. The regulation on the European Border and Coast Guard was adopted in line with the expectations of the summit at an express rate. Today we are talking about its implementation, the full operationalisation of the European Border and Coast Guard at all those sections of the external border where Member States cannot deal with the restoration of proper control for objective reasons. On the other hand, unfortunately, we have seen a great deal of commitment to continuing the reform of the asylum system, it can be said, in line with the old logic of the European Union's conduct, in line with the logic that brought about a very serious political crisis on the ground of the relocation system, which was adopted as a type of pilot project in September 2015. This case continues, just as every important issue in the European Union lasts for more than 6 months. We're at the next stage today. I think the Polish position on this issue is very clear. We want an efficient way of managing the external borders, we want an effective EU asylum system, but we do not agree to any interference, any external impact on refugee or migration policies, which should be fully the responsibility of the Member States and should not be shared in any way, even indirectly, with interference or with the responsibility of the Community institutions. In the area of security, work on strengthening the European dimension of the fight against terrorism continued, but with greater enthusiasm due to the very tragic events that have taken place in Europe. On the one hand, this is a very important submission concerning more intensive data exchange: intelligence, data from services. There is a dilemma ahead of us as to whether these data should primarily serve the Community institutions, or whether they should serve the Member States, whether cooperation in this extremely sensitive area should be based more on cooperation between States or more on the Community institutions. As far as employment is concerned, we have been faced with the continuation of an extremely broad and fully supported digital agenda by Poland. None of the legislative aspects ended during the Dutch Presidency, but the work was expected to take place at the right pace, with the right intensity. We see in the Digital Agenda not only the necessary element of updating the shape, mechanisms of the common market, but also the condition that the market for services and the market for the exchange of goods is really complete at European level. We have a serious problem with that today. In particular, the services market has great difficulties to be a functional market. This is one of the aspects of the Community market which is the most underdeveloped. The Digital Agenda will certainly allow it to be implemented more easily in the European Union. On the other hand, with regard to initiatives which had much less support from the Polish government, the Dutch Presidency decided to continue and intensify the work on the Posting of Workers Directive. This House has also decided very well to raise the allegations of subsidiarity and proportionality of this legislation, which was proposed by the European Commission before the previous act was implemented in this area in the EU. In our deepest conviction, the Posting of Workers Directive, which has unfortunately made some legislative progress during the Dutch Presidency, is a directive which is not so much intended to protect the social rights of posted workers - there is no controversy between us, this government is extremely attached to the protection of social rights, labour rights - its deferred, and in fact the main objective is to introduce a very serious injection of protectionism in the services market, on the Community labour market. The Dutch Presidency is also the launch of a highly theoretical but important discussion on the future financial framework of the European Union and thus preparations for the ongoing mid-term review of the multiannual financial framework for the period 2014-2020. In this case, thanks to the Ministry of Development, Poland has recreated a group of friends of cohesion. It is one of the fundamental dimensions of Polish policy in relation to the European Union budget, alongside agriculture. We have managed to create a very clear Visegrad Group document on the mid-term review of the multiannual financial framework. We are an active participant in the discussion on European added value, on efficiency, on new financial instruments in the budgetary perspective. We are open to discuss any reform of the multiannual financial framework. One thing is certain: we do not want this reform to be a pretext for eliminating or weakening treaty policies, and cohesion and agricultural policy are one of these. We are certainly not prepared for new challenges, which should also be implemented by the European budget, to be met at the expense of those policies which are crucial from a Polish point of view. Running a little alongside the period we are talking about, the current state of play of the debate on how the multiannual financial framework should look after the mid-term review seems to meet Poland's expectations that this reform should be cautious and not take place at the expense of those elements of the European Union's budget, which are important from the point of view of Poland and Central Europe. As far as the euro area is concerned, there is little progress here. We haven't had any breakthroughs. In particular, no progress has been made with regard to the sharing of deposit liability with regard to joint protection, a common guarantee for deposits. The Dutch Presidency focused on the logic contained in the five presidents' report - strengthening macro-economic conditionality, strengthening the European Semester, which seems not to have been controversial at this level of generality. In particular, there was no controversy in Central Europe, which still seems very attached to clear fiscal rules also at Union level. But a lot of very intense, very important things were happening in the area of energy and climate from the Polish point of view. We still have an open discussion on the renewal of the reform of the decision on the transparency of energy contracts. We still have an open threshold problem from which contracts should be notified and possibly checked for compliance with EU law. On the other hand, we also have an open discussion about the mechanism itself and the point at which the agreements concerned should be assessed by the Community institutions. We have an open process of reform of the Security of Gas Supply Regulation, although at the time we have seen significant progress in this discussion. The discussion on the shape of regions, which should jointly and severally bear responsibility for possible crises in the supply of gas at the external borders of these regions, is not closed in particular from a Polish point of view. Of course, a significant part of the attention was given at that time to preparations for ratification from Paris, which has accelerated, also in the Polish Sejm it is visible in today's agenda. It has radically accelerated the moment of the breakthrough in China, the United States and India in terms of their willingness to enter quickly, but this goes beyond the Dutch Presidency. In the area of climate policy, we have seen significant progress, although not meeting Polish expectations, in shaping new rules for the ETS market, a new market for CO2 emission allowances. We have seen clear progress, although still not meeting our expectations, in those sectors of the economy which are not covered by the ETS and which are covered by the EU thresholds, the EU's ambition to reduce CO2 emissions. We are also not fully satisfied with the compromise on reducing other dusts: nitrogen oxides, sulphur oxides. I am talking about the NEC directive, in which Poland managed to overcome a radical reduction of ambition, in particular, in terms of reducing ammonia, which could possibly impact agricultural production in Poland, although the levels of reductions and levels of EU ambition in this matter are still far too high. This is the reason why we expect that the final shape of costs and the final distribution, the burden sharing of all climate policy, both in the ETS and in the non-ETS, will be further negotiated so that these costs and burdens are expressed in a much more balanced way than we are now being offered. To sum up, the time between January and June was certainly intense. Holland didn't waste any time. Not all initiatives were in line with Polish expectations, but many of them were definitely so - I spoke about them in particular at the beginning. Following Brexit's experience, it began in principle also under the Dutch Presidency, today we expect Brexit's experience to be read so that decisions that could be taken even more quickly and simply, by alienating the interests of certain groups, regions, Member States of the European Union, are taken far more consensually. I am thinking, in particular, of decisions on the new asylum order and the new rules on the European Union's climate policy. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
