ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u340	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am very pleased to be able, on behalf of the Committee on European Union Affairs, to present the report for the second time and present a draft resolution of the Sejm on the participation of the Republic of Poland in the work of the European Union in the period January-June 2016. Information from the Council of Ministers on Poland's activities during the Presidency of the Netherlands was adopted by the Committee for European Affairs on 5 July this year, and on 6 July it was forwarded to the High House. The rapid transmission of information by the government to the Sejm allows us, in Parliament, specifically in the European Union's Committee on Affairs, to discuss the events of the Presidency almost immediately after its completion. The government's information, as in previous years, was prepared as a result of cooperation between all ministries and central offices, and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs is responsible for its presentation. It is already the 25th half-yearly report of the Council of Ministers for the Sejm and Senate of the Republic of Poland from the participation of the Republic of Poland in the work of the European Union during the successive periods of the Presidency. The Commission for European Union Affairs considered the information on 19 July 2016. The information on behalf of the Council of Ministers was presented by the Secretary of State in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Mr Konrad Szymański. This is the first information for which the current government is fully responsible. The Minister presented the course and achievements of the Dutch Presidency through its priorities and key issues from Poland's point of view. The Dutch Presidency focused on the following issues: migration and international security, innovation and employment, stable European finances and development of the euro area and climate and energy policy. Migration cases have been an important issue and are still an issue for the next Presidency. In the first half of 2016, migration was one of the most important topics. In March, an agreement was reached with Turkey to reduce the migratory pressure towards Greece. The issue of reform of the asylum policy system is still on the table. Poland, however, has a negative assessment of some of the European Commission's proposals. Another aspect of migration policy was the establishment of a European Border and Coast Guard. The Dutch Presidency has set out a very ambitious plan to adopt a regulation within 6 months. The Commission for European Union Affairs dealt with this regulation in February this year, in April the Sejm adopted a resolution on the migration policy of Poland. The second priority of the Presidency, namely innovation and employment, was focused on the internal market of the European Union, taking into account its digital dimension. The Digital Agenda received Poland's support, but the Posting of Workers Directive was not supported and both the government and the Sejm strongly opposed the proposed solutions. Another challenge was climate and energy issues from the point of view of Poland's interests. Work continued to fully implement the Energy Union and thereby strengthen the European Union's energy security. Work on the signing and ratification by the Union of the Paris Agreement to the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change of December 2015 was crucial for Poland. Last week, on Friday 30 September, the Council of Ministers introduced a draft ratification bill. The result of the British referendum was a very important subject, albeit not planned. In July this year, when we discussed the report at the committee meeting, the Union had just started preparing for the UK's exit process from the European Union. The position of the 27 Member States adopted on the day following the first European Council stressed that until the end of the United Kingdom's membership of the European Union, all rights and obligations should be fully respected. It was crucial to emphasise the lack of agreement of the 27 Member States of the Union to deal selectively with rights and obligations in the common market. As President of the European Union Committee on Affairs, I would like to convey to the High House that the Dutch Presidency is also a time for the active work of the European Union Commission during the Dutch Presidency of the Council of the European Union. The Commission held 55 meetings. It should be noted that the committee is one of the most busy parliamentary committees, has examined 431 European Union documents, has adopted 10 opinions, including 2 opinions on EU documents. In the case of one of the documents relating to the posting of workers, the committee found that the draft legislative act was not compatible with the principle of subsidiarity. The Sejm then adopted a resolution which declared the project incompatible with the principle of subsidiarity. The Commission also held discussions at its meetings on trafficking in human beings in the digital age, the European Commission's health and food protection priorities with the EU Commissioner for Food. health and food safety, recommendations to Poland in connection with the implementation of the 2016 European Semester, progress in the implementation of the Energy Union. The Commission also gave an opinion on many candidates for posts in the institutions of the European Union, including the appointment of Jarosław Starzyk as a representative of the Republic of Poland to the European Union. The Commission has also adopted an opinion in which it critically commented on the proposal for the selection of members of the European Parliament in the general direct elections. Finally, I would like to mention a positive opinion on the declaration proposed by the Marshal of the Sejm: security, borders, renewed institutions. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u342	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The period of the six-month presidency of the Netherlands in the Council of the European Union deserves deep attention, for two main reasons. This idea will be further developed by referring to further developments that were planned or unplanned during the Dutch Presidency. Indeed, such an unplanned event was the settlement of the British referendum. However, it is worth to return to February 2016, when the government made a significant contribution to the development of a compromise solution in an area particularly important for Poland - in the area of social benefits and freedom of movement of people. It is worth noting that Poland opposed the indexing of child benefits and the possibility of temporarily suspending the right to benefits for citizens of the European Union and the United Kingdom. In this regard, the government has been successful, unfortunately the referendum in the UK has cancelled all these arrangements. The March agreement with Turkey has already been mentioned here, but I would like to emphasise once again the government's attitude, which has consistently pointed out that the assessment of the involvement of individual European Union countries in this problem should take into account the differences arising from objective premises and differences in past experience and ways of implementing asylum policy. The key demand of Poland is to maintain the principle of voluntary participation in the activities of the European Union, rejecting the compulsory mechanism of redistribution or relocation. The Dutch Presidency has also made some efforts to combat terrorism. It has led to the adoption of an initiative aimed at improving the exchange of information in the area of justice and home affairs. The European Union Trust Fund for the stabilisation and prevention of illegal migration of the population in Africa has been launched. Poland has signed a founding agreement and a donor contribution certificate of EUR 1.1 million. Poland also declared financial support to a regional trust fund, in response to the Syrian crisis, amounting to EUR 3 million. The best proof that this is not the case is, for example, the indicated financial resources involved by the Polish Government. The most important event on this subject was the meeting of foreign ministers and six Eastern Partnership countries in Brussels in May. In the context of the southern neighbourhood of the European Union, it is also worth mentioning Polish engagement. Our allies were fulfilling the decision on military support for the coalition's efforts to fight the Islamic State - four F-16 fighters and a land component, about 200 soldiers, for exploratory and training activities. In the area of the common security and defence policy, Poland actively participated in the development of the newly designed CSDP instrument, so-called. an initiative to build capacity for security and development, promoting its use in support of the European Union's eastern partners. An important element, unfortunately, which is a problem for not only Poland, but many countries of the European Union, is the directive on the posting of workers in the framework of the provision of services. But here also Poland has shown a proactive attitude. Parliament, which is worth noting, has mobilised other parliaments, we have launched the so-called initiative. A yellow card. A separate issue is how, unfortunately, today, these initiatives of the yellow card are treated by the European Commission. This, of course, also requires some reflection. On the other hand, this directive will have to continue to evaluate the committees either way and Poland will continue to oppose the negative provisions of this directive. An important element of the Presidency is energy security, including, of course, energy security in our country. On 16 February, the so-called. the winter package for security of supply. It fulfils the commitments of one of the two priority pillars of the Energy Union for Poland: security of supply based on the principles of solidarity and completion of the internal energy market. The second element on which we have also spoken out loud in the European Union is the issue Nord Stream 2. In March 2016, the Prime Ministers of Poland, the Czech Republic, Slovakia, Hungary, Lithuania, Latvia, Estonia, Romania and Croatia sent a letter to the President of the European Commission calling for concrete action by the European Commission, informing in this letter of the expected negative effects of the pipeline on energy security, also calling in this letter to ensure that the project is compatible with European Union law, in particular with the third liberalisation package. In conclusion, this Presidency period has been a very difficult period for Europe. Under these difficult conditions, the government of the Republic obtained, you can say, as much as could be obtained. He spoke decisively on matters which were crucial for Poland and in which it was necessary to protest, but he also supported those issues which were important and required solidarity for the whole European Union. That is why we are in favour of accepting information from the Council of Ministers about the participation of the Republic of Poland in the work of the European Union in the period January-June 2016. Thank you very much.

The Free and Solidarni Circle shares this thesis. The consequence of the lack of a vision of strategic policies within the European Union itself from the point of view of Polish interests is the search for long-term solutions to the migration crisis. Divergences between the EU countries on this issue remain, positions are extremely different. Europe should be a catalyst for innovation and employment, but it is increasingly stuck in endless agreements between countries and the growing Brussels bureaucracy. Thus, the European Union is starting to lose its momentum in this area, increasing its distance to global innovation leaders. It is difficult to accept for success the Council's recommendation to set up national productivity councils, which was adopted by the Council of the European Union on 17 June 2016. First of all, the recommendation may remain unanswered by the Member States. Even when national productivity councils are set up, it is hard to believe that they will have a strong impact on the growth of innovation, on the skills of workers and on the rigidity of labour markets and product markets, or on the further improvement of the Economic and Monetary Union. Similarly, the work towards the full implementation of the Energy Union, thereby strengthening the European Union's energy security and increasing the efficiency of the functioning of the internal energy market in the European Union from the point of view of the benefits for final consumers, is extremely inadequate. Detailed solutions in this area determine Poland's place on the energy map of Europe. The government should therefore develop a precise position on this issue. Part two of the government information contains a broader description of the actions undertaken in the various thematic areas and draws attention to the demands and initiatives aimed at implementing Polish interests, in respect of both key projects that positively affect the development of the country and the elements with potential risks. On the contrary, we see that a certain way of thinking, for example on the role of the single market for the economies of the Member States, in one or the other country - it does not apply to Poland or any Central European country with certainty, but it does apply to many countries - a certain way of seeing the common market is very, very similar to this mechanism, to looking at the common market, which has led a large part of the British opinion to decide to leave the European Union. Today we need reform more than consolidation, certainly more than consolidation of the EU institutions. This is a strange assumption, given that in a crisis situation we should rather care about really open, really profound reflection on what went wrong. I have the impression that some of the voices that have been voiced in this Chamber - in different tones, sometimes in a tone of fear, sometimes in a tone of mockery - and which this element of the Union's deep reform excludes, does the European Union a favour, defending today's status quo, defending the Union as we have today: a highly imperfect Union, a Union that is far from the public's growing group of Member States of the European Union. This does not apply to Poland. Defending this status quo, we're zooming in on this organization's decomposition scenario. If we really want this organization to be well and to be renewed, we must care about reform. And here we come to a much more political issue. What role do we anticipate for our country in this process? The reform process of the European Union will take place one way or another. We also had a lively parliamentary dialogue in the area, the format of the Weimar Triangle. The six largest Member States of the European Union are being consulted tomorrow. This is an important aspect of the future of the Union because, with the UK leaving the European Union, we will have a new political situation, and we will have to find new guarantees for non-euro area countries in the common market. Finally, the Visegrad Group. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u377	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I was honored to present to the High House a draft law on the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, print No 880. [[I don't know if it's an honor.]] [[You should be ashamed of yourself.]] The Constitution of the Republic of Poland contains regulations concerning the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. However, in order to fully define the powers and responsibilities of these judges, constitutional provisions are not sufficient. For this reason, the legislator first developed the rules on the status of judges of the Tribunal in the Constitutional Court of 1997 and subsequently in the subsequent laws of 2015 and 2016. The legal solutions relating to this matter have been included in addition to the rules on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Court, and thus in addition to the issues which, in accordance with the article. 197 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland was to be governed by the law. The report of the panel of experts on the Constitutional Tribunal set up by the Marshal of the Sejm shows that the system of the Constitutional Tribunal could regulate at least three normative acts in parallel. First, the Constitution of the Republic of Poland to a wider extent than it does today. Secondly, the Act, but indeed limited only to the organisation and mode of work, not the Constitutional Court Act. Thirdly, of course, the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, especially as regards the procedure for the selection of judges of the Tribunal, since they are elected by the Sejm in accordance with the Constitution, and the question of the procedure for the selection of judges does not, after all, cover the organisation of the tribunal. That's what the expert team report says. The drafter took into account the position developed by the panel of experts for the Constitutional Tribunal and concluded that the status of a judge of the Constitutional Tribunal does not fall within the category of the organisation, or even more so in the procedure of proceedings, as also stated by the Constitutional Tribunal in the justifications of its decisions. In the explanatory memorandum to the case of 9 March 2016, K 47/15 reads, quote: The procedure for submitting candidates for judges of the Constitutional Tribunal is undoubtedly not in the mandate to determine the procedure before the Tribunal in the Act of Procedure. As indicated above, the appointment of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal is an integral part of the procedure before the Sejm and no stage of the procedure takes place before the Constitutional Tribunal. It cannot, however, be accepted that this issue is one of the matters reserved for the Act on the organisation of the tribunal. Such an extension of the interpretation of the concept of "organization of the tribunal' is not confirmed by the other provisions of the Constitution, which contain an authorisation to act as a body. In particular, it is excluded by Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with the examination procedure referred to in paragraph 1 of this Article. 7 Constitution, in which the organisation and principles of operation of the National Bank of Poland and the detailed rules for the appointment and cancellation of its organs were treated as two separate areas of statutory regulation. These spheres cannot be identified. End of quote from the Constitutional Court's justification. This allows the projector to assume that it is appropriate to create a separate bill concerning the status of judge of the Constitutional Tribunal. The importance of this procedure is reinforced by the fact that, in order to determine the status of judge of the Constitutional Tribunal, the constitutional provisions are insufficient, which may hinder the exercise of powers by members of the constitutional body of the State. The authors of the project stress that the position of judge of the Constitutional Tribunal differs from that of judges of general courts, administrative courts or judges of the Supreme Court. Despite some similarities, the function of a judge of the Constitutional Tribunal has such jurisdiction that his status should be regulated separately and, to the smallest extent possible, refer to the proper application of the provisions of the Supreme Court Act. However, since the criteria for the selection of a judge of the Constitutional Tribunal are identical to those of the Supreme Court, there is nothing to prevent certain standards laid down for judges of the Supreme Court taking due account of the different features of the function of the Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal from being adapted to this Act. In particular, judges of both authorities cannot be distinguished in terms of their qualifications and characteristics, which they should have not only as candidates for the office concerned, but also in the exercise of their constitutional and statutory duties. In particular, it is about the requirements for legitimisation of a certain level of substance, impartiality, which guarantees independence, as well as an adequate state of health necessary for the exercise of office. The above mentioned circumstances have decided that the group of Members has proposed to regulate the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal in a separate act in a comprehensive manner. However, references to the duties and rights of Supreme Court judges have not been completely waived. In this way, the proposed law is in line with the setting of a high standard concerning the status of judges of the highest judicial authorities in Poland. This is the implementation of the recommendation of the panel of experts to the Constitutional Tribunal, who considered that, I quote: It is permissible under the existing Constitution to adopt a second parallel law concerning the Constitutional Tribunal. Its adoption could be based on the provision of the Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 2 of the Constitution, which expressly states that the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal shall be provided with working conditions and remuneration corresponding to the dignity of the office and the scope of their duties. These conditions are not part of the organisation of the Constitutional Tribunal or the procedure before the Constitutional Tribunal, and they are difficult to include in the Constitution or in the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm. Therefore, in addition to the basic law concerning the Constitutional Tribunal, which should be a rather narrow law on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal, it would be possible to adopt this Act, this time on the working conditions and the remuneration of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. End of quote from the expert report. For these reasons, the act submitted by the group of Members therefore concerns the rights, obligations and rules of liability of the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. It also characterizes the service relationship of the judge of the Tribunal. The draft law consists of five chapters. In Chapter 1: General provisions, the drafter repeated the constitutional standards concerning the size of the body, as well as the principle of the Sejm's choice for a 9-year term and the characteristics of the candidate for the judge's position. In the latter context, the draft refers to the qualification requirements needed to take up the position of Supreme Court Judge. This is a solution that has worked well in practice and ensured that suitable candidates for the role of judge of the Constitutional Tribunal have been selected. At the same time, it does not impose on the Sejm an excessively narrow framework limiting a significant circle of people who could be selected. For these reasons, the drafter considered that it was not necessary for the new law to regulate the selection of candidates for judges of the Constitutional Tribunal in a way different from that previously. With regard to the procedure for the selection of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal itself, the drafter sent a reference to the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm. This is, firstly, confirmation of the constitutional competence of the Sejm to elect judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, and secondly, it is also the implementation of the conclusions of the report of the panel of experts on matters of the Constitutional Tribunal, who explicitly stated that, I quote: the Sejm - in accordance with Article. 194 - elects judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, suggesting that matters relating to the electoral procedure should be subject to a rule of law - p. 41 and 42 expert team report. An important part of the project is the rules on the status of the person elected as judge of the Tribunal. The project sets out the requirements for taking a vow and maintains the principle of taking a vow to the President. The proposed provisions implement the explanatory memorandum of the Constitutional Tribunal for its judgment of 3 December 2015 (sign. K 34/15). I quote: The vows made to the president in accordance with the article. Article 21 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Constitutional Tribunal Act is not merely a solemn ceremony of a symbolic nature referring to the traditional inauguration of the term of office. This event has two important functions. First of all, it is a public promise by the judge to act in accordance with the vows he has made. In this way, the judge declares his/her personal responsibility for the impartial and careful performance of his/her duties in accordance with his/her conscience and respect for the dignity of his/her office. Secondly, the taking of the oath allows the judge to begin office, i.e. the execution of the mandate entrusted to him. These two important aspects of the vow show that it is not just a solemn occasion, but an event with concrete legal effects. For this reason, the involvement of the President in taking the oath from the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal elected by the Sejm should be placed in the sphere of the exercise of the powers of the head of state. It was a quote from the explanatory memorandum to the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal. The consequence of the above-mentioned position is, firstly, that the refusal to take a vow is equivalent to the resignation of the judge of the tribunal, secondly, that the service relationship of the judge of the tribunal is established after the oath is taken. The Judge shall, after taking the oath, be placed immediately in the Court for the purpose of taking up his duties and the President of the Tribunal shall assign him cases and create conditions for the performance of his duties. The proposed wording of the provisions on vows and its consequences is also confirmed by the position expressed in the report of the panel of experts on the issues of the Constitutional Tribunal. In Recommendation No.4 they stated: In the Polish tradition of government and law, vowing plays an important and special role as a sign of loyalty and a form of assurance of compliance with obligations. This justifies the maintenance of an obligation to take an oath by a judge of the Constitutional Tribunal. The refusal to take the oath should be equivalent to the resignation of a judge of the Constitutional Tribunal. Chapter 1 of the draft also contains a provision on the independence of the Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal. Art. 5 of the project extensively regulates requirements related to impartiality and apoliticity. It also sets limits on the performance of additional activities by the judge of the Tribunal. The proposed standards are intended to guarantee the integrity of the performance of the duties of judge. In this respect, a particular role has been assigned to the President of the Tribunal, who, in accordance with the Article, has been assigned. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 4 may object in writing if the taking up or continuing of employment or other occupation hinders the performance of duties, undermines the dignity of the Tribunal's office or undermines confidence in its impartiality or independence. This regulation responds to the recommendation of the panel of experts on matters of the Constitutional Tribunal. Art. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 The 5th draft sets out an additional obligation for the judge to act in accordance with the Code of Ethics of the Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal. Such a code is to be adopted on the basis of separate legal provisions. The issue of the adoption of the Code of Ethics is an expression of the implementation of the recommendation of the panel of experts on the issues of the Constitutional Tribunal, which recognised: An essential condition for the functioning of an independent constitutional court is to ensure the apoliticality of judges. The Constitution of the Republic of Poland shall be established in this respect by the minimum necessary. The requirements of apoliticity of judges should be specified at the statutory level. Furthermore, it would be advisable to draw up an ethical code at the Constitutional Court. The expert group also stressed: In accordance with the article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 3 The Constitutional Tribunal judges must not belong to a political party, trade union or conduct public activities incompatible with the principles of the independence of courts and the independence of judges during the period of office. Although there is a view in the literature that the obligation to refrain from the conduct and participation of judges in political activities only concerns the period of active performance of functions, it should also be referred to the time when the judge is at rest and therefore enjoys certain privileges. In this context, it should also be seen as particularly gross due to the political intensity of the statements and behaviour of certain retired judges, including the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. The statutory restrictions on the period of rest, in which a person remains a judge and should keep the right distance to the political sphere, seem to be reasonable and desirable. The specific involvement of a judge of the Constitutional Tribunal at rest in political activities should in any event result in his disciplinary responsibility. The question of the possibility for a judge of the Constitutional Tribunal to carry out certain public activities is reflected in the proposed law. The draft in separate chapters regulates the status of retired Judges and Judges. With regard to the duties and rights of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, it shall lay down certain requirements and limitations for which a judge, including the retired, may be held liable by disciplinary action. The second chapter of the proposed law sets out the principles of liability and the immunity of the judge of the Tribunal and his personal immunity. The proposal includes both rules relating to criminal liability or deprivation of liberty as well as matters relating to disciplinary liability. The drafter has set out in detail the rules for the waiver of immunity to the Tribunal. The decision in this case was left to the General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. This corresponds to the regulation contained in Article. 196, according to which - a quote from the Constitution: the Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal may not, without the prior consent of the Constitutional Tribunal, be held liable or deprived of liberty. The Judge may not be arrested or arrested, except in the act of apprehending him if his detention is necessary to ensure the proper course of the proceedings. The arrest shall be notified without delay to the President of the Constitutional Tribunal, who may order the immediate release of the detained person - the end of the quote from the Constitution. Art. Article 10 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the draft shall indicate the grounds for disciplinary responsibility. It shall be implemented before the Constitutional Tribunal for violation of the law, failure to act as a judge of the Tribunal's office, violation of the Code of Ethics of the Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal or other unethical conduct likely to undermine confidence in its impartiality or independence. The draft fills the current gap in the rules on disciplinary action against the Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal by a more detailed description of the procedure. For this reason, a group of Members, in addition to regulations such as the compositions of a disciplinary court, the absence of a cassation complaint against a disciplinary decision given at second instance or a catalogue of disciplinary penalties, which are reprimanded, reprimanded, reduced remuneration of 10% Up to 20% for a period of up to 2 years, the submission of the judge to the Tribunal of its own motion shall lay down the rules for initiating the procedure and giving him the run. The detailed regulation of disciplinary proceedings shall be implemented by Recommendation No 10 of the Expert Group on. issues of the Constitutional Tribunal. The experts in the report stressed that - I quote: The establishment of transparent, logical and functional rules of disciplinary action against judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, both ruling and at rest, is a necessary requirement of a democratic rule of law in which public authorities and their officials act on the basis and within the limits of the law. The properly regulated way in which judges of the Constitutional Tribunal are held accountable also meets an essential element of the legitimacy of the constitutional judiciary. In addition, consideration should be given to the additional moral requirements which are imposed on those exercising public functions, including, above all, judges. The mechanism for bringing judges to disciplinary action should first guarantee the authentic independence of the Constitutional Tribunal and the independence of its judges - p. 77 expert reports, end of quote. The draft provides for disciplinary action against judges of the Constitutional Tribunal only within the framework of the Constitutional Tribunal. This is in line with the recommendation of the expert report that - I quote: Disciplinary action against judges of the Constitutional Tribunal should be closed to the Constitutional Court, which means the inadmissibility of involvement in the procedure for the enforcement of disciplinary liability of other external entities. At the same time, it must be pointed out that a particular responsibility in disciplinary proceedings lies with the President of the Constitutional Tribunal, which, apart from other circumstances, justifies the need to establish the term of office of that position. However, the closure of the disciplinary responsibility of the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal in the Court of First Instance does not mean that there is no possibility for third parties to make an appropriate notification. Such notice, which should be noted, shall not initiate any disciplinary procedure against the judge but merely constitute a preliminary element of the validity of which, autonomously, while fully respecting the principle of independence of the Constitutional Tribunal, the President of the Constitutional Tribunal shall decide. It must be emphasized with all its power. It is recommended to establish, at the statutory level, an entity, for constitutional reasons, which is particularly predisposed to submit an appropriate notification. It is the President of the Republic of Poland, who, due to his duties as guardian of the Constitution, has an obvious role here - the end of the quote from the report. For this reason, in Article 2 (1) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013, the Commission is empowered to adopt delegated acts in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council concerning the placing of plant protection products on the market and repealing Commission Implementing Regulation (EU) No 540/2011. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the project was identified by a group of bodies entitled to notify the President of the Constitutional Tribunal of the decision by a These persons include both the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal and the authorities obliged to uphold the Constitution, i.e. the President of the Republic of Poland, and the rule of law - the Attorney General. It should be stressed that the projector has established a requirement for cooperation for the latter two entities. The proposal provides that the President may submit a notification of the offence at the request of the Attorney General. It is an expression of cooperation, but also of balance of power. The power of the President of the Republic of Poland to submit a notification of an offence by a judge of the Constitutional Tribunal is the implementation of the article. 126 of the Constitution, in which the Constitutional legislature entrusted the President of the Republic of Poland with the task of upholding the Constitution. This task also relates to the statutory position of the President of the Republic of Poland as the subject of taking the oath from the person elected as Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal. The President by law becomes the guarantor of the conduct of such a judge according to law and ethics. The power of the Prosecutor-General to submit an application to the President of the Republic of Poland concerning the offence of a judge of the Constitutional Tribunal is, however, linked to the political position of that body as a public-interest entity. Importantly, the abovementioned entities shall not affect the final decision to initiate disciplinary proceedings or the outcome thereof. Once again, this must be emphasised with all force - this is an internal matter of the Constitutional Tribunal. It is up to the disciplinary officer appointed by the President of the Constitutional Tribunal by drawing a draw that a preliminary examination of the charges brought against the judge of the Constitutional Tribunal and of the decision to initiate proceedings should be made. This procedure is described in Article. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 The proposed law. The proposed solution refers to the provisions of the Supreme Court Act and refers to the provisions of the Law on the System of General Courts and the Code of Criminal Procedure for Unregulated Matters. Thus, for the first time, the question of disciplinary action in the case of a judge of the Constitutional Tribunal is regulated more fully, which gives clear and clear rules on how to conduct such proceedings and, on the other hand, provides procedural guarantees for a judge of the Constitutional Tribunal who is blamed. These issues have not yet been clearly identified. In Chapter 3, the drafter defined the rights and obligations of the judge of the Tribunal. The rule has been maintained that the working time of the Judge of the Tribunal is defined by the dimension of his duties. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: The 18 projects identified the indicators needed to calculate the basic remuneration of the Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal and the remuneration of the President and the Vice-President of the Tribunal, as well as the rules for determining that remuneration. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 19 of the project confirmed the right for the judge of the tribunal permanently residing outside Warsaw for free accommodation in Warsaw and the reimbursement of travel expenses and the allowance for separation with the family in accordance with the Act on employees of state offices. An important group of provisions are Article. A draft laying down issues such as prohibitions on the performance of a particular activity by a judge of the Constitutional Tribunal, the obligation to make a declaration of the economic activity of the judge's spouse or the obligation to make a statement of property. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: The 23th draft was governed by the obligation of the judge of the Constitutional Court to make a statement on his or her assets and to appoint the President of the Constitutional Tribunal in this respect. This provision provides for the disclosure of the statements of property of the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal and the obligation to publish them in the Public Information Bulletin of the Constitutional Tribunal. This is an element of preference for the transparency of people with high public functions. The proposed amendments concerning the disclosure of the declaration of economic activity carried out by the spouse of the judge of the Constitutional Tribunal and the disclosure of claims of property, including the property of a separate judge and the co-ownership of the spouses, aim to prevent corruption from occurring and tightening up existing solutions. The transparency of property statements enables them to be more effectively controlled. Consequently, it is intended to ensure that the courts of the Constitutional Tribunal, who are public officials, are in the right and interest of the State to act. Such action shall conform to the standards set out in the case law of the Constitutional Court. The designer is aware that the obligation for the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal to provide information about their assets and to make it public interferes with the right to privacy, including information autonomy. However, it should be borne in mind that Article. Article 61 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland guarantees citizens the right to obtain information about the activities of public authorities and persons performing public functions. In its judgment of 8 October 2001, the Court of First Instance held that: K 11/01 The Court stressed that, I quote the justification of the Constitutional Court: the legislator has the freedom to determine the scope of restrictions on persons performing public functions, which can be shaped differently at different times due to the worsening of corruption and the negative social response. They should remain in a rational relationship with the public interest to which they are intended and their scope should be commensurate with the level of that interest. The limits imposed on those exercising public functions cannot therefore be considered in terms of restrictions on the freedoms and rights of those persons, but should be regarded as a means of ensuring the proper functioning of public institutions. End of quote from the justification for the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal. On the other hand, on the grounds of the judgment of the Constitutional Court of 21 October 1998, the order of 21 October 1998. K 24/98 follows, and I quote: No citizen is obliged to apply for or to perform a public function, and knowing the consequences of this fact in the form of making public a certain range of information belonging to the privacy sphere, it takes a self-contained and conscious decision based on a positive and negative impact account, calculating certain limitations and discomfort associated with interference in private life. End of quote from the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal. It follows from the above that by deciding to stand as a judge of the Constitutional Tribunal and then acting as a judge, the person concerned accepts the conditions limiting his or her right to private and family life. The rules do not impose arbitrary obligations here. The proposed solution is the realisation of values such as transparency and transparency of public life. It is intended to ensure that the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal perform their duties in a fair and impartial manner, thereby implementing the constitutional powers of the Tribunal as an independent judicial authority. The refusal to make such statements shall be equivalent to the resignation of the Judge of the Tribunal. So there is a choice. The Group of Members considered that the content of the provisions concerning certain prohibitions on the conduct of business by judges resulting from the Supreme Court Act better took into account the nature of the status of such entities. Therefore, Art. 20 of the bill repeats the solutions contained in the Law on the Supreme Court in respect of bans on the conduct of business by judges. In this way, judicial independence is better guaranteed. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Article 20 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 The three draft Members' group proposed that the Court's judge should transfer the proceeds of holding shares or shares in a commercial company to the public objectives it has designated by 31 March of the year following the year in which it obtained them. The failure to comply with this obligation is equivalent to the resignation by the judge of the Tribunal. This is to express the participation of the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal in the pursuit of the common good and to prevent the temptation to pursue private interests in a situation of potential conflict between the interests of the company whose shares the judge has and the interests of the state and citizens. Rejection means resignation. The proposal also laid down rules for the expiry of the term of office of the Judge before the end of his term of office. Art. Article 26 (2) (b) 1 of the draft provides that, in the case of first, the death of the judge of the tribunal, secondly, the resignation by the judge of the court of office, thirdly, the conviction of the judge of the tribunal by a final judgment of the court for an intentional offence prosecuted by a public charge or a deliberate tax offence and fourthly, the final judgment of the judge of the tribunal of office. In the mouth. Article 2 26 the authorities empowered to terminate the mandate have been identified. In a separate chapter 4, the designer proposed provisions on the state of rest. The principle that the judge of the Tribunal shall retire after the end of his term of office has been maintained. However, the designer made an additional reservation in this regard. As a model of the Supreme Court law, the projector introduced a requirement for the President of the Tribunal 6 months before the end of the 70th year of age of the Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal to be able to perform the duties of judge due to the state of health. Such a certificate shall be submitted to the President of the Tribunal at the age of 70 by 31 January of each year until the end of his term of office. If a candidate for a judge of the Constitutional Tribunal is to have jurisdiction such as that of the Supreme Court, it should be considered that this obligation applies not only to the moment of the election but also to the entire term of office. It is also about legitimizing the right state of health. In addition, the designer, like the Supreme Court, warned that the judge of the Constitutional Tribunal who did not retire after 70 years of age could not act as President or Vice-President of the Tribunal. This is due to the willingness to relieve these people of their administrative responsibilities and to enable them, because of their great legal knowledge and experience, to serve the good of the state and society. The draft sets out the rights and obligations of the retired Judge and the consequences of the breach of those obligations. The importance of apoliticity of the retired judge, which, like the President-in-Office, cannot be part of a political party, a trade union or a public activity incompatible with the principles of the independence of courts and the independence of judges, has been stressed. The duty to uphold the dignity of the judge of the tribunal has also been highlighted. Art. 31 of the project lists cases of loss of the status of judge of the tribunal at rest. They are similar to those relating to active judges. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 33 The drafter separately regulated the issue of the apoliticality of the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal at rest and the prohibition of their activities incompatible with the principles of the independence of the courts and the independence of the judges. The rules for the taking up of employment at the university and the subordinateness of the Judge of the Constitutional Tribunal resting on the Code of Ethics of the Judge have also been laid down. The Constitutional Tribunal. The proposalor decided that, due to the new way of regulating the matter concerning the tribunal, the transitional, adapting and final provisions would be included in a separate law - the introductory provisions. Such a legislative procedure is justified by the fact that from the existing one law, which regulated various issues relating to the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, it was distinguished in accordance with the wording of the article. 197 of the Constitution, and in line with the recommendations of the expert panel on the Two acts of the Constitutional Tribunal. In order to ensure consistency of regulation and to introduce new legal solutions efficiently, a separate law implementing these provisions has been designed. The reference to the Introductory Act is set out in Chapter 5 entitled: Final provisions. In this chapter, the legislator referred to the proper application of certain provisions of the Law on the Supreme Court in matters not governed by the law and relating to the rights and obligations of judges. This approach is intended to prevent loopholes in the law. The Act orders and develops constitutional regulations concerning the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. The new legislative act will not give rise to any doubt as to the powers and responsibilities of the constitutional judge. A separate act on the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal will highlight their role as members of the constitutional body of the State, which is the Constitutional Tribunal. The entry into force of the Act on the Status of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal will not, in principle, impose an additional burden on the State budget. Finally, the subject matter of the proposed law is not covered by European Union law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u511	Mr. Marshal! Mr Fellowships and Mr Fellow Members, During the meeting of the Committee on Public Finance on 5 October, the first reading of the Parliamentary draft law on amending the Act on Tax Administration and the Act on Social Insurance, print No 842. On behalf of the Members of the applicant, the objective of the proposed amendments to the above-mentioned amendments is set out. the laws, the way in which it is implemented, and the discussion has been held. The aim of the project is, generally speaking, to increase the effectiveness of the enforcement of the obligations relating to income tax on individuals and insurance premiums by the Ministry of Finance and the Social Security Office, as well as access to data relating to taxpayers and insured persons, their verification and use for the development of analyses and forecasts concerning the above mentioned. civil duties. The preparation of such forecasts is quite understandable; it is part of tax, health, economic and social policy. And the proposed records are intended to facilitate and guarantee greater access to the data available to the Minister of Finance and the Social Security Office by exchanging this information between the Ministry of Finance and the Social Security Office. During the work in the committee, doubts raised the security of personal data, which, according to some of the members of the committee, are threatened by the flow of information from the Ministry of Finance to the Social Security Office and vice versa. The representatives of the Ministry of Finance, the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy and the Social Security Office present at the meeting do not share these concerns, claiming that today such access to data takes place in both ministries, as well as in the Social Security Office. In accordance with the Personal Data Protection Act and other provisions, the confidentiality of personal data is sufficiently protected by law. In this respect, and also from the analysis provided to us by the Sejm Analysis Office, these proposed amendments respect the provisions of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, Directive 95/46/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council, and the Regulation of the European Parliament to replace and enter into force from 2018. The protection of personal data is a high priority in European documents, but as the documents cited here show, there is a consent to the processing of data in the public interest in the exercise of public authority. This prohibition on the processing of health data is particularly strongly emphasised in European documents, e.g. with regard to disability. However, in this case, the data contained in the Social Insurance Institution's documents concerning insured persons are, as an exception, covered by European documents, and in this respect are also excluded from the prohibition on the use of data. Two amendments were tabled during the discussion. [[Three.]] Two corrections. [[And the date of entry into force?]] The first amendment concerns the fact that the Tax Administration Act is currently in the vacathio legis period. If this provision concerning the obligation to transfer data by the Ministry of Finance to the Social Security Office had been left in this law during the period of vacathio legis, this could not have been implemented. That is why we have proposed that this provision be transferred as an article. 8c to the Act of 21 June 1996 on tax offices and tax chambers - at the stage of the committee's work. This is consistent with legislative practice, there can be no reservations here. We also proposed an amendment concerning the date of entry into force of the law. We proposed to shorten the date of entry into force of the Act until the day following the publication period. These amendments have been voted by a majority of committee members, accepted and are included in the committee's work report on this printing. I ask for their support in further work in the Sejm. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u512	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u513	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice a position towards the parliamentary draft law amending the Act on Tax Administration and the Act on Social Security from Print No 842. This bill was sent to the Committee on Public Finance for first reading. The first reading and examination of this project took place on 4 October this year. The purpose of this bill is to ensure that information can be exchanged between the Social Insurance Institution and the Ministry of Finance to the extent that it is possible to carry out analyses, forecasts and forecasting models and to verify the data held by both institutions. Such data flows will allow for the harmonisation and clarification of the data of payers and insured persons who are simultaneously taxable persons. In the current legal state, data held by the Ministry of Finance and the Social Insurance Institution may be made available to each other in the form of anonymous data sets or aggregated statistics. Following the adoption of the provisions proposed in this Act, it will be possible to make available the data in question about the insured persons, taxpayers and payers concerned in order to allow for the correlation of the individual data held by the tax offices and the Social Insurance Institution. Wide access and analysis of data should enable the Ministry of Finance to assess the financial consequences for citizens, businesses and public finances in certain regulatory projects. The exchange between the two institutions of these data will enable the Ministry of Finance to fulfil its obligations under Article more effectively. 74 and 174 Public Finance Laws, which require the Minister of Finance to exercise control over the level of sovereign government debt and the deficit of the public finance sector, which includes the Social Insurance Fund. It is worth noting that this draft law does not have any financial implications for the state budget and the budgets of the local government. The European Union's position on the subject of this law has been widely discussed by Mrs Gabriela Masłowska. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support this bill in the vote, together with the amendments adopted at the meeting of the Committee on Public Finance. Thank you very much.

The Act only refers to the performance of statutory tasks, the creation of analyses, forecasts and forecast models and the verification of data. Only with this control can we determine whether the transferred data is adequate to the tasks performed by the individual institutions, and only then are we able to determine whether the condition referred to in the Constitution is fulfilled that these data should only be transmitted for certain purposes, including in the case of certain necessary public tasks, and whether the transferred data are actually necessary for the performance of a given task, or whether it is not so that someone, using the fact that he has access to the data, will use these privileges of access for completely different purposes which are incompatible with what the Act says. The project also does not address technical issues at all, precisely how technically, physically the data of persons who will be transferred between offices will be protected. The penalty is set at the above level in order to ensure consistency with the applicable rules on penalties for offences of a similar nature. In particular, Article. 55th Act on the Institute of National Remembrance, i.e. the offence of denial of the Holocaust, or Article. 133 of the Criminal Code, i.e. public insulting of the Polish people or the Republic of Poland. Another element is that penalties in the form of fines or restrictions on freedom are proposed for acts committed unintentionally. Then, as in any criminal proceedings, so in this case, the court will have a wide catalogue of tools that allow for less severe criminal repression than imprisonment to be applied to the perpetrator. Another element is that criminal liability is excluded where the perpetrator of an act is allowed in the context of artistic or scientific activity, that is a countertype. Here's another piece. It's a design art. 45a. Another element, the penalization of behaviours defined in the proposed article. 55a is also present in European Union law, here we are talking about the Framework Decision of 28 November 2008 on combating certain forms and manifestations of racism and xenophobia by means of criminal law. Then, when the act is committed abroad or the perpetrator is a foreigner, the investigation will be conducted according to the same rules as in any other criminal case with the foreign element. Polish concentration camps, Polish labour camps - such information comes from foreign sources, are disseminated by various media, press and television. For this reason, not only does a forgery picture of history arise, but above all such statements are in the interest of the Republic of Poland and the Polish nation. Therefore, it is good that finally now, after 27 years so-called. The government of Law and Justice decided to take these matters into its own hands and presented the Sejm with a very, in my opinion, a good solution, which, above all, gives legal possibilities to secure national interests in this context. This is about criminal protection, as well as civil law protection, because so far all those who have falsified Polish history... Creating a sign of equality between the perpetrators of World War II, the Third German Reich, and those who were victims, especially Poles and the Polish nation, is a clear injustice. The lack of decisive action in this matter deserved to finally submit legal solutions to the Polish Parliament. For this, I would like to thank the government very warmly that such solutions have been submitted, along with solutions that are in line with the European Convention on Human Rights, as well as with European Union law, because this has been reflected and confirmed in the case law of the European Court of Human Rights and the European Court of Justice. This is very important, because today, by virtue of these decisions, it will be possible to prosecute the perpetrators of this type of crime, because the solution put forward by the government precisely as a new one defines such a crime, which is connected with telling the Republic of Poland about acts which had no realization, reflected in facts and shape an untrue picture of war reality. Such acts are subject to prosecution by law. Polish courts, which will decide according to their jurisdiction, which is defined by European law, as the Minister also mentioned here today, will be able to pursue justice. This is very important. Government submission is also a very important decision in the context of the protection of war cemeteries and graves, because it opens up opportunities for various institutions, including NGOs, to use public funds to protect such places of national memory. It is a solution that also shapes the right civil attitudes, but in my opinion it is also important for the protection of these places of national memory. I am convinced that this decision presented by the government will be adopted unanimously by the Polish Parliament. ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u595	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Today, the Joint Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on Foreign Affairs have carried out the first reading of the Government's draft ratification act adopted in Paris on 12 December 2015. The Paris Agreement to the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change, drawn up in New York on 19 May 1992. High Izbo! The agreement adopted in Paris on 12 December 2015 offers an opportunity to reduce the rate of carbon dioxide concentration in the atmosphere so that, according to the adopted mathematical model, the increase in global average temperature is not greater than two degrees compared to the pre-industrial period. In principle, the Paris Agreement involves all countries in the world, including the largest pollutant issuers such as China, the United States or India. The Paris Agreement allows action to be taken to reduce the concentration of carbon dioxide, but taking into account the economic specificities of individual countries, and represents a return to basic findings, to the spirit of the climate convention. The Polish contribution, I will tell you more, the contribution of the Minister of Environment, Professor Jan Szyszko, is a provision in this document that takes account of the carbon balance and removal of carbon dioxide. Since, as agreed in the Paris Agreement, it enters into force after ratification by countries with a minimum emissions of 55% Global emissions can be said to have already entered into force, as 60 countries have ratified them, including the largest carbon issuers - China, the United States or India. 30 September Council of the European Union The Environment, the Assembly of Environment Ministers of the European Union, adopted a decision on ratification and yesterday the European Parliament held a debate. Thus, negotiations on the implementation of this agreement will begin at the COP conference in Marrakesh, which will take place at the beginning of November. So Poland should be present there, because absent people do not have a voice. We must therefore urgently ratify so that by 7 October we can send a notification to our country for this conference. High Izbo! It should be noted that the 1992 Climate Convention is a framework, sets out the objective of stabilising the concentration of greenhouse gases in the atmosphere and lays down general principles for the cooperation of States Parties to achieve it, while the Kyoto Protocol of 11 December 1997 provides for specific actions and targets for reducing and reducing greenhouse gas emissions by the parties listed in Annex B to that Protocol. The amendment to the 2012 Protocol, drawn up in the Doha with the objectives of reducing emissions for 2013 and 2020 did not enter into force because it had not been ratified by sufficient number of parties. High Izbo! The Paris Agreement is a new opening, a way out of the deadlock, opening the way for implementation of the climate convention after almost 25 years. Thus, firstly, it ensures that emissions are reduced by the solidarity of all countries and not only of developed countries. As climate change is a global problem, it should be addressed by a coordinated, joint effort of all countries. In order to achieve satisfactory results, it is essential to involve all countries, especially the largest economies, in particular reducing the risk of carbon leakage. Secondly, the current system was based on rigid solutions, i.e. any change required the adoption and ratification of the amendments, which led to the need for ongoing negotiations. The Paris Agreement, on the other hand, agreed on more flexible solutions to ensure sustainability in changing circumstances. The Paris Agreement introduces the so-called. the distribution cycle, i.e. national contributions. All participating countries are required to make regular contributions according to their capacity and each subsequent contribution should be higher than the previous one. The renewal of contributions is to take place over a cycle: a global review, communication of contributions, transparency assessment and implementation. Again: another global review, etc. Such a procedure removes the need for continuous negotiations and ratification. Thirdly, the Paris Agreement establishes a single system for the settlement of greenhouse gas emissions and removals, a uniform system for monitoring, reporting and verification. It applies to all parties. The Parties will be required to submit national inventory reports to the Secretariat of the Convention at least every 2 years, to provide data to verify progress in the implementation of national contributions and to pass through expert reviews of the data transmitted. Fourthly, the measures taken to reduce emissions depend on the country-specific circumstances and should be defined at national level. Climate protection measures should be supported by economic incentives, i.e. clear market mechanisms of a predictable and stable nature, in order to ensure the conditions for investment, including private sector investment in emission reduction measures. Fifthly, the Paris Agreement takes into account the fact that the fight against global warming affects very many groups of people, affects entire communities, affects their rights, affects their development opportunities. Therefore, the agreement establishes an obligation to cooperate with a view to better education in order to build public awareness, public participation and universal access to information on this subject. Furthermore, the agreement considers key issues such as the transition of the labour market to create decent, suitable jobs and respect for human rights. High Izbo! The Joint Committees: Environmental Protection, Natural Resources and Forestry and Foreign Affairs carried out work on the draft print bill No 886 submitted after the first reading and requested the adoption of the draft law contained in the report of the Joint Committees in print No 900. In the course of the work, the Commission adopted proposals for the Legislative Office on the wording of the title of the bill and the article. 1 of this draft and adopted the amendment of the Group of Members to the article. 2 with a proposal to shorten vacathio legis. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the joint committees, I ask the High House to adopt a draft law in print No 900. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The Paris Agreement to the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change, drawn up in New York on 19 May 1992. According to the Intergovernmental Panel on Climate change - the IPCC, in order to avoid catastrophic climate change, needs to reduce the increase in global average temperature below 2°C. This requires stopping CO2 concentrations in the air below 450 ppm - CO2 particles per million air particles - which is only possible if global CO2 emissions are reduced by 40%. by 2050 in relation to 2010 However, the content of the Treaty does not leave any illusions. The agreement does not contain strict emission reduction commitments by major issuers. Unfortunately, the provision to keep the temperature rise below 1,5.2°C compared to the pre-industrial era can be interpreted by the EU as taking a more ambitious approach than the previous one. Although the proposed treaty, which will be open for signature by States at UN headquarters from 22 April 2016, has no specific and binding provisions as in the Kyoto Agreement, there are significant risks. Namely, the European Commission is always more ambitious than the rest of the world. The way the Union interprets the new treaty is the biggest threat to Poland. Supporters of radical decarbonisation have failed to maintain concrete, binding and ambitious provisions that could have a real impact on the reduction of global emissions. From a global perspective, emissions will continue to grow consistently. Many entries in the COP21 Paris Agreement have been diluted or deleted, including. at the initiative of the US and China. Stopping global warming is our debt to future generations. Despite strong reservations, Modern is in favour of ratification.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u614	The Paris Agreement to the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change adopted in Paris on 12 December 2015 establishes multilateral rules on climate protection after 2020 under the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change, established in New York on 9 May 1992. The agreement calls for a reduction of greenhouse gas emissions as soon as possible, recognising that this will take longer for developing countries. Action to reduce CO2 emissions will be taken by all parties to the agreement while respecting their specificities and socio-economic opportunities. The Paris Agreement sets the objective of keeping global temperatures below 2°C up to the end of the century. It also states that during this period we should strike a balance between greenhouse gas emissions and their absorption, among others. through the woods. Action to reduce CO2 emissions will take place, among others. by introducing new technologies or by increasing the share of renewable energy sources in the energy balance. In turn, the balancing of emissions will take place, among others. on the way to growing forestation. This last entry was introduced into the contract thanks to the determination of the Polish delegation. Countries - the parties to the agreement - will be able to implement measures to reduce greenhouse gas emissions while respecting their socio-economic specificities. The Climate Agreement will enter into force when ratified by at least 55 States - signatories to the Climate Convention - which are responsible for 55% global emissions. It is expected that it should apply by the end of 2020 at the latest. The agreement at the Paris Climate Summit does not impose any specific commitments on its signatories, it does not talk about decarbonisation and allows the balancing of emissions by forestation. This is an argument for Poland for the announced efforts to renegotiate the EU climate package. Countries supporting the agreement at the Conference of the Parties to the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change have advocated a binding objective of reducing the global average temperature increase by 2°C and have promised to make every effort to reduce it to 1.5°C. However, they did not impose on themselves the means to achieve this. The document also includes a mechanism to compensate for losses caused by climate change, which could lead to an increase in sea levels, droughts and desertification. The mechanism of loss and Damage set up at the Warsaw Summit (COP19) was also clarified, although without specific commitments. It provides support for developed countries to combat losses in developing countries, i.e. funding by richer countries to eliminate the damage caused by climate change in poorer countries. There were no references to human rights due to resistance from the United States, Norway and the United Kingdom. The document also states that developed countries should, and must not, make an effort to finance energy transformation. The draft does not include references to decarbonisation, taxes or penalties for greenhouse gas emissions, but it is voluntary to introduce emission trading mechanisms. COP21 calls only on countries to create more ambitious plans to combat climate change. They are to report regularly on their progress from 2018 every 5 years. The PSL Club will support this bill.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-10-05-sejm-27-2.u696	There are big and small wars. These big ones change the world, these little people. In early September this year. In Włocławek Penitentiary, the exhibition was officially opened in Włocławek. The Nazi death camp Auschwitz-Birkenau. The exhibition was created thanks to the cooperation of the Regional Director of Prison Service in Bydgoszcz colonel. Jack Guest and Dr. Piotr Cywiński, director of the Auschwitz-Birkenau State Museum. It is worth noting that the exhibition in the framework of cultural and educational activities was prepared by the settlers of the Penal Institute in Włocławek. The opening of the exhibition was accompanied by numerous speeches, including a lecture by Prof. Dr. Hab. Witold Stankowski. The exhibition consists of several parts. The first is the passage through a tunnel filled with photogrammats from the Auschwitz-Birkenau concentration camp, passing into the structure of a cattle wagon which was transported to the camp. The main part of the exhibition is a camp barracks and an appeal square with barbed wire. The impressions are heightened by the sounds of the camp: the screams of the oppressors, barking dogs at people, crying children and silent prayers of prisoners. The section with afishes, which contains information about the history of the camp, experiments carried out by Nazi scientists and everyday life in the camp, brings millions of innocently murdered people into a traumatic world during World War II. The recreated gas chamber, above which the inscription is written: the bath is very sad, reminding that no one came out alive. The employees of the Penal Institute in Włocławek made sure that the exhibition was enriched with multimedia, thanks to which viewers can especially touch the shocking realities that prevailed in the camp. For organizing the exhibition, congratulations are due to the director of the Penal Institute in Włocławek colonel. Mark Opperman, as well as the educators who supervised the project. The uniqueness of the exhibition is due to the fact that it was prepared by prisoners in prison. Convicts voluntarily volunteered to perform design elements. Many of them were impressed by this work. Reliving the tragedy of that time, during the reconstruction of camp events, led the prisoners to analyze their own life and moral reflection... Initiating similar undertakings is absolutely necessary and desirable, because the worst prison of people is a closed and hardened heart, and the greatest evil is despair.
